# Research state

Generated by `scripts/research_state.py --write` from
[research_state.json](research_state.json); gate core-270 fails on any
difference. Edit the registry, never this file. Historical cases, collections
and analyses keep their original bytes; this file states which of their
conclusions stand.

## Evidence classes

| Class | Establishes | Does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| mathematical claim | a statement under its assumptions and named proof origin, or an explicit open question | machine checking; a separate machine-checked claim carries that |
| exact finite certificate | exact facts about the enumerated or witnessed objects, re-derived by its gates | any object outside the certificate |
| machine-checked proof | the statement as kernel-checked by Rocq from the retained source | a fresh kernel check: the receipt records an earlier compiler run and CI verifies its bytes |
| numerical or stochastic simulation | model predictions at the stated seeds, precision and noise model | hardware behavior |
| circuit-conformance check | ideal circuit semantics: statevector, operator or routed-schedule equality | noise suppression or any hardware performance |
| preregistered hardware inference | the registered endpoint decisions on the named acquisitions | mechanisms the design does not isolate, and other devices or calibrations |
| preregistered emulator control | the registered exact outcomes of a noiseless emulator twin on the named acquisitions | device noise and any hardware behavior; a hardware inference carries those |
| exploratory reanalysis | descriptive or post-selected summaries of retained data | confirmation; it motivates a prospective test |
| proposed experiment | a design with its question, controls and falsifier | any result; a QPU run needs its own approved case |
| withdrawn claim | the record of a statement the evidence does not support | the statement itself; its replacement or reason is named |

A checksum gate verifies retained bytes, green CI verifies the enabled checks,
and neither establishes a theorem or authorizes a QPU run.

- **kingston-mod15-pilot** (2026-10-11T152922.787095Z): Do the hybrid Shor and folded-QPE witnesses beat their exact nulls, and does packing change them against isolated references?
- **kingston-folded-qpe-ladder** (2026-10-12T055624.091217Z): How do delay, no-feed-forward and coherent two-control arms change terminal accuracy against the archived dynamic QPE?
- **kingston-correction-first** (2026-10-13T020454.599853Z): Does known-label correction beat measured-bit correction by more than 0.01 on both odd labels?
- **kingston-correction-second** (2026-10-13T022433.333775Z): Repeat of the correction decision under a later calibration.
- **fez-ripple-atlas** (2026-10-13T025444.603480Z): Do the 84 box-kite bright-minus-dark contrasts and the depth-4 class return appear on hardware?
- **fez-rank-sectors** (2026-10-13T032011.664435Z): Do the 8 sector contrasts and 12 pulse-difference contrasts of the rank-sector family appear on hardware?
- **kingston-wide-control** (kingston-wide-control-result): What do a nominal 156-qubit Ramsey PUB and a 29-replica carpet show about spectators and support violations? Recovered retrospectively.
- **fez-n21-orbit-feedback** (2026-10-13T080216.451663Z): Does the N=21 orbit's measured feed-forward match its exact law, and which mechanism explains the deviation?
- **fez-n21-orbit-window** (2026-10-13T083417.634585Z): How do delay, extra-readout and echo window arms change the fitted orbit phase?
- **fez-patch-window** (2026-10-13T201757.782152Z): Does the orbit's idle window leave a resolved phase on the work qubits under matched Ramsey probes?
- **kingston-coherence-window** (2026-10-15T030748.903241Z): How do a matched idle and a mid-circuit measure-and-reset change the branch-signed work coherence?
- **kingston-rank-sectors** (2026-10-15T030903.235380Z): Do the Fez rank-sector rejections transfer to a second Heron r2 processor?
- **kingston-ripple-atlas** (2026-10-15T031229.371738Z): Do the Fez ripple rejections transfer to a second Heron r2 processor?
- **kingston-corner-baseline** (2026-10-15T070847.644554Z): Do readout, relaxation and Ramsey on Kingston qubits 0-4 and 16 match their reported calibration?
- **kingston-zero-divisor-fringe** (2026-10-15T070853.550532Z): How large is the Design B dark floor off the corner, and which fringe term carries its excess over the model?
- **qi-qx-crosstalk-teleport-reference** (2026-10-16T211635Z-QXsinglenodesimulator, batch job 869574, 10240 of 10240 shots done): Does the analysis recover the ideal law on the noiseless QX emulator: target phases within sampling of zero shift and zero pairwise residual, drivers at 0, and unit teleportation fidelity after the Pauli-frame correction?
- **qi-rydberg-teleport** (2026-10-16T211635Z-Rydberg, batch job 869576, 0 of 1024 shots done): Does the transported-pair teleport circuit, with its bit register and measurements, execute on the Ry emulator? The job failed at execution.
- **qi-tuna17-crosstalk-teleport** (2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17, batch job 869577, 81920 of 81920 shots done): Does driving a neighbor shift a Tuna-17 target's idle phase, do the pairwise shifts add under simultaneous drive, does simultaneous drive change the drivers' own gates, and how faithfully does a Bell pair whose half a SWAP carries teleport Pauli eigenstates?
- **qi-rydberg-x-diagnostic** (2026-10-16T213823Z-Rydberg-diagnostic, batch job 869597, 1024 of 1024 shots done): Does an X on q[0] with no bit register and no measurement, the form of the Rydberg code example, execute on the Ry emulator, separating an unavailable backend from an unsupported circuit, and which outcome character carries q[0]?
- **qi-tuna17-zero-divisor** (2026-10-16T221133Z-zero_divisor_tuna_17, batch job 869643, 81920 of 81920 shots done): What dark floor, bright level and visibility does Tuna-17 give the exact (e1 + e10)(e4 -+ e15) witness across three regions in a Latin square, and does the symmetric contraction law hold?
- **qi-qx-zero-divisor-twin** (2026-10-16T221133Z-zero_divisor_qx, batch job 869645, 10240 of 10240 shots done): Does the platform execute the nine routed Clifford+T zero-divisor replicas exactly on the ideal QX emulator (dark 0, bright 1, fixture 1/2)?
- **qi-qx-coupler-zz-dry-run-0** (2026-10-16T224534Z-coupler_zz_qx-batch-00, batch job 869693, 6144 of 6144 shots done): Dry run, batch 0 of 4: does the ideal QX emulator return vanishing coupler and frequency rates for the 10-qubit Tuna-17 subgraph before any hardware submission?

## Established claims

- **sedenion-determinant-membership** (mathematical claim, algebra). For every real sedenion v=(x0+u,x8+w), det L_v=(s+A+B)^4 [s^2+2s(A+B)+(A-B)^2+4 gamma^2]^2, with s=x0^2+x8^2, A=||u||^2, B=||w||^2 and gamma=<u,w>. A nonzero v is a zero divisor exactly when s=0, A=B and gamma=0.
  Boundary: The determinant factorization is Theorem 3.7 of Koebisu version 2, a preprint; Biss-Dugger-Isaksen supplies the membership and annihilator foundation. A finite rational replay validates the implementation separately from the all-real source argument.
  Proof origin: external-source.
  Assumptions: Real standard sedenions under cd_core's doubling and ordered basis; u and w are imaginary octonions; v is nonzero for zero-divisor membership.
  Evidence: [2512.13002.md](../notes/papers/2512.13002.md), [math-0511691.md](../notes/math-0511691.md), [algebra_circuit_rules.md](../notes/algebra_circuit_rules.md).
  Related claims: sedenion-determinant-exact-replay.
- **sedenion-determinant-exact-replay** (exact finite certificate, algebra). Exact left-matrix determinants match D1^4 D2^2 on 300 seeded integer vectors, 100 seeded rational vectors, e0+e9, e1+e9 and e1+e10. The controls have determinants 256, 256 and 0, and removal of 4 gamma^2 fails on e1+e9.
  Boundary: 403 rational fixtures at seed 20261011 validate the evaluator; the replay establishes facts only on those fixtures. The all-real identity comes from the separate mathematical claim.
  Assumptions: int or Fraction coordinates in exactly 16 dimensions.
  Evidence: [algebra_circuit_rules.md](../notes/algebra_circuit_rules.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-044.
  Depends on: sedenion-determinant-membership.
- **cd-associator-census** (mathematical claim, algebra). The Cayley-Dickson algebra of dimension D = 2^n >= 8 has N(D) = (D-2)(D-4)(D+6)/2 non-associating ordered basis triples; an independent pair has 0 associating completions when D/2 lies in its span and D/2 - 4 otherwise; every 3-dimensional index plane carries a linear functional fixing its associator signs (168 or 72 non-associating triples).
  Boundary: Real basis triples under the cd_core.cd_sign convention; the moving-pair spectrum is the separate claim cd-moving-pair-walsh-proof.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); real basis triples.
  Evidence: [census.json](../evidence/cd_associator_defect_census/census.json), [cd_associator_defect_census.md](../notes/topics/cd_associator_defect_census.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-212, sources-215.
  Related claims: cd-moving-pair-walsh-proof.
- **cd-moving-pair-walsh-proof** (mathematical claim, algebra). For every D = 2^n >= 16, omega(D/2+1, D/4+3, k) = -(-1)^(q.k + [k in E_D]) with q = D - 4 and E_D = {0, 2, 3, D/4+2} + {0, D/2}; the pair has algebraic degree n - 2, and for D >= 32 Walsh support 5D/16, largest normalized amplitude 1 - 16/D and nonlinearity 8 (at D = 16 support 4, amplitude 1/2, nonlinearity 4).
  Boundary: Theorem 5 and its corollary in the census note prove the formula from the certified associator lifting (Lemma 3) and the fixed-pair parity law (Theorem 4); the census checks the formula, both lifting steps and every spectral law from 16 to 4096. No proof assistant has checked the argument.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)).
  Evidence: [cd_associator_defect_census.md](../notes/topics/cd_associator_defect_census.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-212, sources-215.
  Depends on: cd-associator-census.
- **singular-sector-split** (mathematical claim, algebra). (L_i L_j)^2 splits every two-term element into dark, bright and anticommuting sectors with L_a^T L_a eigenvalues in {0, 2, 4} in every dimension; a doubling recursion proves the anticommuting-pair count and the largest two-term nullity D/2 - 4.
  Boundary: Two-term elements only; the de Marrais higher-dimensional comparison and any hardware echo contrast remain open.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); two-term elements e_i + s e_j.
  Evidence: [singular_sector_dynamics.md](../notes/singular_sector_dynamics.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-240.
  Depends on: cd-associator-census.
- **hpp-fixed-degree-refutation** (exact finite certificate, algebra). Bounded-degree Boolean interpolation recovers every degree-d promised polynomial on n <= 8 variables from sum_k<=d C(n, k) classical queries, refuting the stated fixed-degree exponential-query claim; squared Walsh data loses the phase the LCU witness carries.
  Boundary: Covers the enumerated variable counts and degrees; a hidden-polynomial advantage claim needs its own oracle, promise and output model.
  Evidence: [cayley_dickson_claim_boundaries.json](../../results/hw_estimate/cayley_dickson_claim_boundaries.json), [CLAIM_BOUNDARIES.md](../../gororoba_quantum/CLAIM_BOUNDARIES.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-041.
- **a5-stiefel-selected-nullity** (exact finite certificate, algebra). 32 selected A_5 Stiefel elements (24 Cayley-transform rational, 8 disjoint-support integer) each have nullity exactly 4: rank 28 modulo 2^61 - 1 and four exact rational kernel vectors.
  Boundary: A selected finite family; it is not a universal nullity theorem for A_5.
  Evidence: [nullity.json](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/nullity.json), [README.md](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-267.
- **a5-d-locus-nullity** (exact finite certificate, algebra). On 1000 exact rational pairs of sedenion zero divisors (200 Cayley-frame a's, each with five b's under independent rational scale factors, |b|/|a| from 0.18 to 508 on the 200 members), the A_5 element (a + b, e_8 (b - a)) has nullity 12 exactly when b lies in V(a) = span{(a1, -a2), (a2, a1)} + Eig_2(a) and 8 otherwise, reproducing Theorem 1.5 with Corollary 7.4 of Biss-Christensen-Dugger-Isaksen (math/0702075); b drawn from Ann(a), b a positive multiple of a and b a positive multiple of (a2, -a1) all fall outside V(a) with nullity 8, and dim Ann(a) = dim Eig_2(a) = 4 with Ann(a) orthogonal to V(a) on every frame.
  Boundary: Known theorems reproduced on sampled exact points: the certificate covers its 1000 pairs only, decomposes neither D_(5,8) nor D_(5,12), and says nothing about ZD_12(A_5) outside H_5^perp or the bracket-axis limits.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/d_locus_nullity/result.json), [README.md](../evidence/d_locus_nullity/README.md), [math-0702075.md](../notes/math-0702075.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-323, core-324.
- **a5-stiefel-pfaffian-reduction** (mathematical claim, algebra). For pure a, b in A_4, L_(a,b)^2 is the real form of (L_a + i R_b)(L_a - i R_b) and L_a + i R_b is complex skew-symmetric, so |det L_(a,b)| = |Pf(L_a + i R_b)|^4; every A_5 Stiefel element is a zero divisor exactly when the degree-8 polynomial Pf(L_a + i R_b) vanishes on a dense subset of the Stiefel set.
  Boundary: Written proof from Moreno's Theorem 2.3, which gate core-660 checks exactly on every e_i and e_i + e_j of the 30 pure coordinates (an identity of degree 2); the density step uses lower semicontinuity of rank. It reduces the A_5 question and does not decide it.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); Moreno math/0512517 Theorem 2.3: L_(a,b)^2 = [[A, -S], [S, A]] for pure a, b.
  Evidence: [pfaffian-identity.json](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/pfaffian-identity.json), [README.md](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/README.md), [math-0512517.md](../notes/math-0512517.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-660.
- **a5-stiefel-pfaffian-identity-test** (exact finite certificate, algebra). At 96 seeded points of the A_5 Stiefel variety over F_p, 48 for each of the two largest primes p = 1 mod 4 below 2^61, drawn through the isotropic parametrization z = a + i b, z' = a - i b, rank(L_a + i R_b) = 14 and rank L_(a,b) = 28 modulo p, so Pf(L_a + i R_b) = 0; 16 A_6 Stiefel controls read full ranks 32 and 64 and 16 non-Stiefel A_5 controls read 16 and 32.
  Boundary: Exact modulo-p ranks at the recorded points only. By Schwartz-Zippel a Pfaffian whose restriction is nonzero modulo p vanishes at one uniform point with probability at most 26/p, so the record makes a generic counterexample improbable without proving universality; the draws are pseudorandom and a modulo-p rank bounds the rational rank from below only.
  Evidence: [pfaffian-identity.json](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/pfaffian-identity.json), [README.md](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-660.
  Depends on: a5-stiefel-pfaffian-reduction.
- **a6-stiefel-witness** (exact finite certificate, algebra). The retained integer A_6 Stiefel element (pure halves, a.b = 0, |a|^2 = |b|^2 = 62) has an invertible left multiplication: det L_v is 810824905836647058 modulo 2^61 - 1 and 486680426 modulo 10^9 + 7, so not every A_6 Stiefel element is a zero divisor.
  Boundary: One witness; the 24 Cayley-transform and 7 other integer elements are further full-rank points of the same family.
  Evidence: [nullity.json](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/nullity.json), [README.md](../evidence/moreno_stiefel/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-267.
- **a6-stiefel-generic-invertibility** (mathematical claim, algebra). On V = V_2(R^31), the unit orthogonal pure pairs (a, b) in (Im A_5)^2, L_(a,b) is invertible on an open, dense, full-measure subset; the zero-divisor Stiefel elements form a closed measure-zero semialgebraic subset of dimension at most 58.
  Boundary: Written proof, not machine-checked; the exceptional subset's exact dimension, components and geometry, and the A_5 question, remain open.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); V_2(R^31) is a connected real-analytic manifold of dimension 59; a real-analytic function not identically zero on a connected real-analytic manifold vanishes on a closed set with empty interior and measure zero (arXiv:1512.07276); det L_(a,b) is a polynomial in the coordinates and is nonzero at the normalized integer witness.
  Evidence: [math-0512517.md](../notes/math-0512517.md), [stiefel_generic_invertibility.md](../notes/stiefel_generic_invertibility.md).
  Depends on: a6-stiefel-witness, a6-stiefel-witness-rocq.
- **signed-kernel-certificate** (exact finite certificate, algebra). L_(e1+e10) annihilates (e4 - e15)/sqrt2 exactly, and with B(t) = (L_e1 + L_e10 + t L_e2)/(2 + |t|) the dark success is t^2/(2+|t|)^2 and the dilation overlap t/(2+|t|); the block encoding's selector-00 block equals B(t), and the signed readout flips sign under |t| and changes under a one-branch selector.
  Boundary: Exact and statevector facts plus a priced FakeFez compile; no hardware visibility.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/signed_kernel_probe/result.json), [signed_kernel_probe.md](../notes/signed_kernel_probe.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-273.
- **cd-sign-row-degree** (mathematical claim, algebra). For every n >= 2 and m != 0, the sign row f_m(x) = [cd_sign(2^n, m, x) = -1] of L_{e_m} has algebraic degree exactly n - 1, so octonion left multiplications are Clifford and every sedenion and higher one carries a sign of degree 3 or more.
  Boundary: Written induction from the cd_sign recursion (R), the commutator lemma and the two-point lemma; scripts/cd_phase_degree.py checks every identity it uses and the statement on every row through n = 8. The machine-checked claim cd-sign-row-anf-rocq proves the statement in Rocq for every n against the tower sign sigma, which the tower basis-sign bridge ties to cd_sign.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); recursion (R) and Lemmas 0 and 1 of docs/notes/left_mult_sign_rank.md.
  Evidence: [cd_phase_degree.md](../notes/cd_phase_degree.md), [left_mult_sign_rank.md](../notes/left_mult_sign_rank.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-320.
- **cd-right-row-degree** (mathematical claim, algebra). For every n >= 1 and m != 0, the right sign row h_m(x) = [cd_sign(2^n, x, m) = -1] of R_{e_m} has algebraic degree 1 when m is a power of two and max(1, bitlen(m) - 2) otherwise, independent of n: the recursion (R) splits h_m into the slices h'_m(y) + xi for m in the low half, xi for m = 2^(n-1), and the left sign row f'_(m')(y) one level down for m = m' + 2^(n-1) with m' != 0.
  Boundary: Written induction (Proposition 4 of docs/notes/cd_phase_degree.md) on the three right-row slices and the sign-row degree; scripts/cd_phase_degree.py checks the slices cell by cell and the degree on every m through n = 8, and the machine-checked claim cd-sign-row-anf-rocq proves the statement in Rocq for every n.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); recursion (R) and Lemma 0 of docs/notes/left_mult_sign_rank.md; the sign-row degree of claim cd-sign-row-degree one level down.
  Evidence: [cd_phase_degree.md](../notes/cd_phase_degree.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-320.
  Depends on: cd-sign-row-degree.
- **cd-associator-phase-degree** (mathematical claim, algebra). For every n >= 3 and distinct nonzero i, j < 2^n, the associator phase g_ij of W_ij = L_{e_i e_j}^dagger L_{e_i} L_{e_j} has algebraic degree exactly max(1, bitlen(max(i, j)) - 2), independent of n: each phase splits as A(y) + c xi over the top index bit with c = N(i') + N(j') + N(i' ^ j'), a pair with an index in the top half has A = g' + delta_u + delta_v + const with u != v, and the census adds 3 2^(b-2) (2^(b-1) - 1) pairs of degree b - 2 at bit length b (84, 360, 1488, 6048, 24384 at dims 16 to 256).
  Boundary: Written induction (slice Lemma D, involution Lemma A, two-point Lemma B); scripts/cd_phase_degree.py checks every identity on all ordered pairs and the statement through n = 8, and the machine-checked claim cd-associator-anf-rocq proves the statement in Rocq for every n. Walsh spectra within one degree, which the degree does not determine, stay with scripts/associator_walsh_classes.py.
  Proof origin: repository-written.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)); recursion (R) and Lemmas 0 and 1 of docs/notes/left_mult_sign_rank.md.
  Evidence: [cd_phase_degree.md](../notes/cd_phase_degree.md), [left_mult_sign_rank.md](../notes/left_mult_sign_rank.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-320.
- **associator-cubic-normal-form** (exact finite certificate, algebra). Each of the 360 degree-3 dim-32 associator phases is l1 l2 l3 + q with l1, l2, l3 linearly independent linear forms on F_2^5 and deg q <= 2, so W_ij = D_q U_A^dagger (CCZ x I) U_A; the cubic part takes 15 values, 24 pairs each.
  Boundary: Dimension 32 only; degree-4 and degree-5 phases at dims 64 and 128 are not examined.
  Assumptions: cd_core.cd_sign doubling convention (a, b)(c, d) = (ac - conj(d) b, da + b conj(c)).
  Evidence: [normal_form.json](../evidence/ccz-reduction/normal_form.json), [cubic_core_certificates.json](../evidence/ccz-reduction/independent_package/cubic_core_certificates.json), [README.md](../evidence/ccz-reduction/README.md), [README.md](../evidence/simulation-notebook-audit/README.md), [RETENTION.md](../evidence/ccz-reduction/independent_package/RETENTION.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-278, core-284.
- **a5-nullity12-gap-extended-precision** (numerical or stochastic simulation, algebra). At the three nonzd-axis witnesses {e_p, 0} of D_(5,12), 12 seeded near-rank-20 points found by alternating projection from starts at scales 0.03 and 0.003 and refined at 110 digits to sigma_21/sigma_1 < 1e-40 keep their float64 relative distance from the {a, 0} family cone (4.28e-4 to 2.47e-2) at three significant digits while lying 1.51e-3 to 2.68e-2 from the witness, and 64-perturbation float64 clouds at scale 1e-2 span 16 directions.
  Boundary: Floating and extended-precision arithmetic at seed 20261023, 110 digits, float tolerance 1e-14, cloud scale 1e-2. A refined point has L_y within 1e-40 of a rank-20 matrix that need not be a left multiplication; the rank-20 cone meets span{L_e} far from transversally, so no error bound places an exact point of D_(5,12) near it, and the record establishes neither membership, nor that the family fails to exhaust D_(5,12), nor a local dimension. A 16-direction span bounds the local dimension from above only. An exact parameterized piece or local equations remain the admission route.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/rank_locus_nullity12_refinement/result.json), [README.md](../evidence/rank_locus_nullity12_refinement/README.md), [rank_locus_two_dimension_gap.md](../notes/rank_locus_two_dimension_gap.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-662.
- **a5-tangent-bound-not-linear** (exact finite certificate, algebra). At all 29 witnesses of the A_5 rank-locus families, the tangent bound T(x) = {y : U^T L_y V = 0} is not inside the closed rank cone R_k = {y : rank L_y <= 32 - k} that contains the cone over D_(5,k): L_y has exact rational rank 32 at six seeded rational points of T(x), integer combinations of a rational basis, at every nullity-4, -8 and -12 witness and rank 20 at the three nullity-16 zero-divisor-axis witnesses, above the cone bound 32 - k.
  Boundary: Exact ranks over the rationals at the recorded points; one point of rank above 32 - k certifies that T(x) is not inside R_k, and the record carries every weight vector, so the certificate is independent of the generator that drew them. The result excludes only a piece whose cone is linear of the tangent-bound dimension: the zero-divisor-axis witnesses, whose local dimension equals the tangent bound on a curved cone, fail the test as well, so a curved piece stays possible.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/rank_locus_tangent_linearity/result.json), [README.md](../evidence/rank_locus_tangent_linearity/README.md), [rank_locus_two_dimension_gap.md](../notes/rank_locus_two_dimension_gap.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-663; replay failure paths checked by core-664.
- **a5-schur-germ-unobstructed** (exact finite certificate, algebra). At the 12 non-zero-divisor gap witnesses of the A_5 rank-locus families, the 9 zero-divisor-family witnesses, the 7 Proposition 8.2 points and the Moreno point, the excess entries of the Schur complement of L_y vanish on the smooth germ of dimension dim T(x) cut out by its independent entries: to O(p^12) at a Hensel-lifted p-adic point (pyadic, p = 2147483647) and through t^63 along four seeded directions in F_p[[t]] (python-flint, p = 2147483647 and 2147483629). At x0 = e1 + e18, whose cone A15 union B17 lies below its tangent bound, they are nonzero at order 2 in both routes with equal leading digits, and at valuation 4 at scale p^2; along seeded velocities at x0 inside A15 and inside A15 intersect B17 they are nonzero at orders 3 and 4 in both routes with equal leading digits.
  Boundary: Exact residues modulo p^12 and modulo p at the recorded seeded directions, whose weight vectors the record carries. A zero residue bounds the order of the obstruction series along the drawn direction from below and admits no local dimension: the series could still be nonzero with order 64 or more, or with a leading form that vanishes modulo both primes or at the drawn weights, which a leading form nonzero modulo p does with probability at most 64/(p - 1) per direction, with weights drawn from F_p^*. A nonzero residue at a gap witness would have refuted a5-nonzero-divisor-two-dimension-gap exactly, and none occurred. The p-adic route is recomputed where pyadic imports, and CI holds its retained rows to the recomputed series route.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/rank_locus_padic_lift/result.json), [README.md](../evidence/rank_locus_padic_lift/README.md), [rank_locus_two_dimension_gap.md](../notes/rank_locus_two_dimension_gap.md), [pyadic.md](../notes/packages/manual/pyadic.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-668; replay failure paths checked by core-669.
- **algebra-hypercomplex-operator-witnesses** (exact finite certificate, algebra). The registered finite exact calculations give derivation dimension 14, chosen-octonion stabilizer dimension 8, occupation dimensions 1/3/3/1 and complex Clifford image dimension 64. The draft ladder pairing fails six stabilizer checks, while the aligned pairing passes all implemented checks. The even five-mode reference has vanishing recorded Standard Model anomaly sums.
  Boundary: Finite convention-bound algebra and a representation reference establish neither a unique Standard Model derivation nor its dynamics, a fundamental scalar field, or device performance.
  Assumptions: The registered cd_core multiplication convention and explicit encoding maps fix the finite basis calculations..
  Evidence: [certificate-diagonal-dephasing-formatted.json](../evidence/hypercomplex-operators/certificate-diagonal-dephasing-formatted.json), [README.md](../evidence/hypercomplex-operators/README.md), [hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md](../design/hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-630.
- **algebra-sedenion-eight-generator-clifford-envelope** (exact finite certificate, algebra). The first eight canonical sedenion left-multiplication operators satisfy the recorded Cl(0,8) relations. Their 256 integer word operators have Hilbert-Schmidt Gram matrix 16 times identity and span M16(R).
  Boundary: The result concerns the named eight-generator associative operator envelope. Sedenion coefficient multiplication remains nonassociative with zero divisors; the left encoding is not a product homomorphism.
  Assumptions: The registered cd_core multiplication convention and explicit encoding maps fix the finite basis calculations..
  Evidence: [certificate-batched-products.json](../evidence/sedenion-clifford-envelope/certificate-batched-products.json), [README.md](../evidence/sedenion-clifford-envelope/README.md), [hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md](../design/hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-631.
- **signed-overlap-kernel-independence** (exact finite certificate, algebra). In the sedenions (cd_core) the signed dilation overlap <L_e2 u|(L_e1 + L_e10 + t L_e2) u>/(2 + |t|) equals t/(2 + |t|) for every real unit vector u, in the kernel of L(e1 + e10) or not, because L_e2 is orthogonal and L_e2^T L_e1 and L_e2^T L_e10 are antisymmetric (exact integer identities); as checks, the certified kernel vector (e4 - e15)/sqrt2, the bright vector (e4 + e15)/sqrt2, e4 and a seeded random vector all read it, and on the kernel support {e4, e15} all 1000 seeded replacement sign tables leave it unchanged. The zero-divisor property is instead the eigenvalue -1 of K = L_e1^T L_e10 on u (K swaps e4 and e15, and its -1 eigenspace is four-dimensional): the cancellation observable cos^2(theta) <w|L_e1 u> + sin^2(theta) <w|L_e10 u> with w = L_e1 u reads cos(2 theta) for the kernel vector and 1 for the bright vector, and one flipped L_e10 sign at e4 or e15, or 755 of the 1000 sign tables, removes its zero at theta = pi/4.
  Boundary: Exact arithmetic over the listed vectors and the seeded sign tables; it says nothing about a device, and a hardware reading of the cancellation observable needs its own approved case.
  Evidence: [signed_kernel_probe_audit.json](../../results/iqm/signed_kernel_probe_audit.json), [iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.md](../design/iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.md), [iqm_probe_audit.py](../../scripts/iqm_probe_audit.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-640.
  Related claims: proposed-signed-kernel-probe.
- **cd-sign-cocycle-degree** (exact finite certificate, algebra). For Cayley-Dickson dimension 2^n, n = 1 to 6 (cd_core), the sign f(p, q) in e_p e_q = (-1)^f(p, q) e_(p xor q) has algebraic normal form degree max(2, n) over the 2n index bits, with C(n, 2) + n monomials of degree 2 and (2^(k - 1) - 1) C(n, k) of degree k >= 3, and degree max(1, n - 1) in q maximized over the left factor p, which Theorem 1 of docs/notes/cd_phase_degree.md proves for every n (n - 1 for every nonzero p, n >= 2); the joint degree and the monomial counts are this certificate's addition. Octonion left multiplication is therefore at most a degree-2 phase, implementable with CZ and Z gates, and sedenion left multiplication reaches a degree-3 phase.
  Boundary: Enumerated for n <= 6; the closed form beyond n = 6 is a conjecture this certificate does not prove. Degree bounds the order of the multi-controlled phases a sign diagonal needs, not the routed CZ count on a lattice.
  Evidence: [signed_kernel_probe_audit.json](../../results/iqm/signed_kernel_probe_audit.json), [iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.md](../design/iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.md), [iqm_probe_audit.py](../../scripts/iqm_probe_audit.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-640.
- **four-support-topology** (mathematical claim, geometry). On support {1, 10, 18, 25} exact sector identities classify every real tuple; the unit base has 96 connected smooth strata; a 1,200-cell CW model with explicit attaching maps gives torsion-free integral homology and a square-zero positive-degree cohomology ideal, and ordinary doubling preserves the five-factor model for every n >= 5.
  Boundary: The first operand stays on the four-coordinate support; other supports, a wedge decomposition, homotopy type and unrestricted topology remain open.

- **echo-pair-coherent-sign** (numerical or stochastic simulation, implementation). Under the retained ibm_fez, ibm_kingston and ibm_marrakesh noise models of 2026-10-15, the echo-matched pair U Z U^dagger Z U and U U^dagger U on the dark input of e1+e10, compiled once at 141 CZ with a symbolic ancilla reflection so both arms share every non-virtual gate, moves amplified - echo by 1e-3 or more positively only for a PREP angle error (+0.012 to +0.019 at 0.03 rad), and every shift of 1e-3 or more from SX over-rotation, CZ local phase and CZ conditional phase, at both signs, is negative (-0.009 to -0.012 for a 0.01 rad conditional phase, -0.024 to -0.029 for a 0.01 rad local phase), so it separates no physical rotation error from a CZ phase. The edges' conditional phases act jointly: the four single-edge shifts sum to 0.24 to 0.33 of the uniform shift, so a per-edge 2m-CZ Y-basis reference, which cancels local phase and SX over-rotation to 8.3e-4 rad, must, under the uniform-phase model, bound every edge to the joint tolerance of 0.0049 to 0.0058 rad, 340 to 501 Y-basis shots per edge and target state at m = 24 for a 40,000-shot pair.
  Boundary: A noise-model prediction (gate depolarizing, thermal relaxation and asymmetric readout from the retained snapshots) with each channel injected alone and uniformly (the conditional phase also one edge at a time) at seed-7 compiles; mixed edge-phase vectors are untested, the reference's slope comes from the model rather than a visibility calibration, and its X-quadrature arms and their share of the shot budget are unsized, the residual after the conditional share is unidentified, since extra depolarizing moves the pair the same way; the matched null holds on Kingston only (the incoherent baseline is 1.3 to 1.5 standard errors at 40,000 shots on Fez and Marrakesh), mixtures cancel, leakage and crosstalk are outside it, and no hardware run of the pair exists.

On the noiseless QX emulator the readout ring-down dry run reports 'not detected' on both series under the preregistered rule: all 10 targets are usable at visibility 1, every |delta(tau)| is at most 13.6 kHz against 4.7 kHz standard errors, chi2(H1) runs 0.25 to 10.96 on 5 degrees of freedom (60.3 on 50 summed over targets), and the largest chi2(H1) - chi2(H2) is 6.02, below the 13.82 threshold. QX returned its 10-character measurement register for circuits declaring bit[20], so the mid-circuit outcomes were absent and the postselected cross-check did not run.

The decision rule was fixed in the cases before submission; the all-shot estimator replaced the postselected one after these data showed the measurement register, which changes no circuit byte and leaves the rule's text unchanged, and on these data the two coincide because no mid-circuit bit returned.

- **qi-qx-hosted-reset-drops-register-phase** (preregistered emulator control, implementation). The hosted QX emulator (QXsinglenodesimulator, backend type 1) does not apply cQASM 3.0's measure-and-flip reset to a qubit in |1> beside a qubit in |->: reset q[0] then H on q[1] reads q[1] = 0 in 2048 of 2048 shots, where measure-and-flip reads 1 in every shot and qxelarator 0.10.0's sqrt(|a0|^2 + |a1|^2) map reads 0, while the same program with X in place of the reset and a reset of an already-zero q[0] read q[1] = 1 in 2048 of 2048. On that input the hosted reset discards q[1]'s relative phase, so a QX law of a circuit that resets a |1>-weighted qubit beside a register carrying relative phase is not the cQASM law.
  Boundary: One batch of three two-qubit circuits on 2026-10-18 under the emulator's deployment that day. It pins the outcome of one input, a |1> reset beside one |-> qubit: it rules out measure-and-flip there and agrees with the qxelarator sqrt map, but it does not establish that map on superposed or entangled reset inputs, where another phase-dropping implementation could differ. It says nothing about Tuna hardware, whose reset is physical. A redeployed emulator can change it.
  Evidence: [qx_reset_phase_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/qx_reset_phase_analysis.json), [qx-runs.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/qx-runs.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-456; source regenerated by core-455; replay failure paths checked by core-457.
  Acquisitions: qi-qx-reset-phase-batch-00.
- **iqm-garnet-signed-probe-coherent-response** (numerical or stochastic simulation, implementation). On the retained Garnet compile of the signed kernel probe (transpiler seed 2, 199 CZ on seven qubits), the first-order response of the measured <Z> on QB7 to coherent gate errors (prx over-rotation and axis error per qubit, residual Z after each CZ per qubit, CZ conditional phase per coupler) vanishes in the odd part at t = 0 for every error and grows with the signed amplitude sqrt(|t|/(2 + |t|)), not with t: the QB6 over-rotation coefficient per unit amplitude is -7.1, -6.9 and -6.2 at |t| = 0.01, 0.05 and 0.25. The even part responds at first order, led by QB11 over-rotation (-8.0 per unit relative over-rotation at |t| = 0.05), residual Z on QB11 (-1.56) and QB12 over-rotation (+1.15), Through the calibration noise model (exact density-matrix probabilities, each error inserted after scheduling) every response shrinks to 5 to 10 % of its noiseless size, unevenly (QB11 over-rotation -0.43, QB12 over-rotation +0.002): QB11 alone would need a 10.5 % over-rotation, three times the 3.5 % its randomized-benchmarking infidelity allows, and all 29 terms aligned at that bound move the even part by at most 0.032, short of the measured circuit-level -0.045.
  Boundary: First-order derivatives of the compiled circuit, noiseless and through the calibration noise model, under a stationary error model (one relative over-rotation and one axis error per qubit, one residual Z per qubit after CZ, one conditional phase per coupler); angle-dependent, time-dependent and crosstalk errors and leakage lie outside it, and the noisy derivatives inherit the calibration model, whose even part the device contradicts. The device's coherent error magnitudes are unmeasured.
  Evidence: [signed_kernel_probe_audit.json](../../results/iqm/signed_kernel_probe_audit.json), [iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.md](../design/iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.md), [iqm_probe_audit.py](../../scripts/iqm_probe_audit.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-640.
  Related claims: iqm-garnet-signed-kernel-probe.
- **algebra-device-operational-falsifiers** (exact finite certificate, implementation). Exact finite controls certify real-left normality at dimensions 2 through 64, the 4/8/4 sedenion Gram sectors, complete filter probability accounting, distinct order and associator references, phase and remote-ensemble invariance, finite embeddings and error-set-specific correction.
  Boundary: The certificate covers its explicit operators, dimensions and instruments. Conditional filtering, a generic complex exceptional point and repeated projections establish no fundamental-physics departure, protected phase, physical squeezing or device benefit.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/algebra-device-conjectures-3/result.json), [hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md](../design/hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-670; active code regression checked by core-671.
- **algebra-device-logical-instrument-bank** (circuit-conformance check, implementation). Seventeen logical circuits implement Design B dark/bright/quadrature controls, coherent/dephased/fixed-order controls, one/two/four-step measurement sequences with unmeasured and reset references, a heralded contraction and dimension-8/16/32 associator spectra; complete branch enumeration agrees with their declared laws within 1e-10 after portable-basis compilation.
  Boundary: Finite numerical conformance without sampled jobs. Portable CX/RZ/SX/X compilation establishes neither native hardware routing nor a scheduler, cost or acquisition result.
  Evidence: [hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md](../design/hypercomplex-operator-evidence.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-671.
- **ascella-signed-coherence-permuted-repeat** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). The approved permuted repeat on qpu:ascella (job 6ef8e8db) ran job-00's 64 circuits, all 64 compiled circuits equal to job-00's, in ANU QRNG order at 16384 samples per iteration: 1048576 samples in 186 s, job-00's duration, for 1.032768 free credits, and all 27 endpoints qualify again. Under the preregistered comparison the outside-block clicks follow the execution sequence, not the setting: per-setting Spearman against job-00 -0.075 (p 0.56), lag-1 autocorrelation -0.653 in the new order (p below 1e-4), compiled-phase change Spearman 0.12 (p 0.35), and the median count grows with the budget (log2 ratio 0.83). The per-setting analyzer residuals reproduce, Pearson 0.974 on X and 0.802 on Y (p below 1e-4), at contrast 0.9719 against 0.9699 on X and 0.9669 against 0.9702 on Y.
  Boundary: Two jobs six hours apart on one device; the alternation's physical mechanism (which acquisition stage alternates) and the configuration error behind the reproducing residuals stay unidentified. Permutation p-values assume exchangeable positions under the null; the budget reading compares medians of two jobs. Equal total durations of jobs six hours apart in different orders assign no marginal time to the further samples, because no record separates acquisition from overhead.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T153028Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-01/results.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T153028Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-01/analysis.json), [submission.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T153028Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-01/submission.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T153028Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-01/raw/result.json), [repeat-comparison.json](../evidence/ascella-order-coefficients/repeat-comparison.json), [README.md](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T153028Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-01/README.md), [ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.md](../design/ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.md), [ascella_coherence_repeat.py](../../scripts/ascella_coherence_repeat.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-ascella-signed-coherence-repeat-replay, core-ascella-signed-coherence-comparison-replay; replay failure paths checked by core-ascella-signed-coherence-comparison-self-test.
  Acquisitions: qd-ascella-signed-coherence, qd-ascella-signed-coherence-repeat.
  Depends on: ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.
- **ascella-signed-coherence-conformance** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). The approved 64-setting signed-coherence request on qpu:ascella (job 739a5271) completed 524288 detected samples in 186 execution seconds for a token-reported debit of 1.016384 free credits, and all 27 preregistered endpoints qualify under the 0.05 coefficient tolerance: the 15 sign-odd and dark coefficients deviate from their ideals by at most 0.0303, the 10 sign-even coefficients stay within 0.0116 of zero, and the mutation difference reads 0.1646 against its ideal 0.1727. The X sign-odd constant (0.4697) and the Y sign-odd sin(2 theta) coefficient (0.4777) exclude their ideal 0.5 while conforming. 6659 of 524288 events (1.27 percent) fall outside the logical block, between 4 and 586 per setting.
  Boundary: One job on one device and calibration; it decides the registered endpoints under their tolerance and certifies no exact ideal. The setting-dependent outside-block share exceeds the case's dark-count bound of about 0.91 percent, so the mechanism behind it, and whether it attenuates the two coefficients that exclude their ideal, is unidentified. Intervals assume independent sampling within each setting, and the job cannot separate drift within its 186 seconds.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T091642Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-00/results.json), [analysis-runner-image.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T091642Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-00/analysis-runner-image.json), [submission.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T091642Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-00/submission.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T091642Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-00/raw/result.json), [README.md](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T091642Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-00/README.md), [ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.md](../design/ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.md), [ascella_coherence_run.py](../../scripts/ascella_coherence_run.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-ascella-signed-coherence-acquisition-replay, core-ascella-signed-coherence-replay.
  Acquisitions: qd-ascella-signed-coherence.
- **hw-mod15-pilot** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). Hybrid Shor mod 15 factor yield 0.4971 [0.4726, 0.5216] against the exact uniform null 0.2969; packed replica 0 differs from its isolated reference by +0.0020 (Shor) and -0.0037 (folded QPE), both inside the 0.03 Newcombe equivalence margin.
  Boundary: The order-4 law is the same with and without the conditional phases, so the feed-forward is untested; the Hoeffding view leaves folded-QPE equivalence unresolved.

- **hw-coherence-window** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On ibm_kingston 4 Re C = 1.653 [1.500, 1.806] at immediate readout; the matched idle moves 4 C by -1.034 [-1.357, -0.711] (Re) and -1.341 [-1.664, -1.018] (Im); the mid-circuit measure and reset adds +0.090 [-0.233, +0.413] and +0.001 [-0.322, +0.324], so no measurement-induced change is resolved.

  Boundary: Transfer of contrast signs; magnitudes differ by device and lane.

- **hw-corner-readout-fault** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). Kingston qubits 0 and 1 misassign the ground state with P(1|0) 0.388 and 0.274 and the excited state with P(0|1) 0.144 and 0.123 against reported readout errors of 0.0088 and 0.0066; qubits 3, 2 and 16 retain 0.491, 0.727 and 0.728 of the excited state after 50 us against 0.718, 0.858 and 0.879 modeled.
  Boundary: One calibration (2026-10-15 00:03:15 PDT); readout crosstalk between simultaneously measured qubits 0 and 1 is not separated from an intrinsic fault.
  Evidence: [collect.json](../../results/live/2026-10-15T070847.644554Z/collect.json), [ZERO_DIVISOR_AND_CORNER_FINDINGS.md](../../results/live/ZERO_DIVISOR_AND_CORNER_FINDINGS.md).
  Acquisitions: kingston-corner-baseline.
- **hw-zero-divisor-floor** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). Off the corner, the Design B dark value is 0.182 [0.146, 0.217] against 0.081 modeled and 0.828 bright; the incoherent contraction (1 - V)/2 = 0.161 carries the floor, with offset and coherent phase terms at most 0.041 in any replica.
  Boundary: Device noise the calibrated model underestimates, not a feature of the algebra; mirror-twin division over-corrects.
  Evidence: [collect.json](../../results/live/2026-10-15T070853.550532Z/collect.json), [ZERO_DIVISOR_AND_CORNER_FINDINGS.md](../../results/live/ZERO_DIVISOR_AND_CORNER_FINDINGS.md).
  Acquisitions: kingston-zero-divisor-fringe.
- **qi-tuna17-zero-divisor-contraction** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 the exact Clifford+T Design B witness of (e1 + e10)(e4 - e15) = 0 has raw P(s = 0) = 0.471 [0.466, 0.475] dark, 0.512 [0.508, 0.517] bright and 0.499 fixture over three regions in a Latin square, an endpoint contrast of 0.042 (4.2 % of ideal, 6.5 % of Kingston's 0.646) with dark below bright by about 13 standard errors; the raw pooled fixture agrees with 1/2 (z = -0.42); the endpoints straddle 1/2 with midpoint 0.4915, so dark + bright - 1 = -0.017 (z = -5.3) rejects the preregistered shared symmetric-contraction model; the region on qubits 0, 1, 2, 4, 5 shows no resolved contrast (0.002, 95 % -0.009 to 0.013); the QX twin reads 0, 1 and 0.490.
  Boundary: One 16384-shot run per circuit on one Tuna-17 calibration; three sequential Latin jobs leave case contrasts exposed to between-job drift. Every dark and bright replica uses 66 CZ and 26 T; each fixture uses 70 CZ and 26 T. Kingston used about 45 CZ and continuous rotations, so endpoint contrasts compare implementations as well as devices. The corner fixture uses selector Q4 while its endpoints use Q2. The preregistered raw tests reject one shared symmetric contraction, not all depolarizing descriptions or any uniquely identified coherent, non-unital or coupler mechanism. The all-zero/all-one readout calibration includes preparation error and omits readout crosstalk under the witness state.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T221133Z-zero_divisor_tuna_17/analysis.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T221133Z-zero_divisor_tuna_17/results.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T221133Z-zero_divisor_qx/analysis.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-330.
  Acquisitions: qi-tuna17-zero-divisor, qi-qx-zero-divisor-twin.
  Depends on: hw-zero-divisor-floor.
- **n21-linear-encoding-noise** (numerical or stochastic simulation, hardware). Under the ibm_fez, ibm_kingston and ibm_marrakesh noise models of 2026-10-16 (12:12, 12:13 and 11:51 -07:00), the coset-15265 encoding of the two-round N=21 n21-feedback diagnostic routes 713 CZ against 1,023 and raises the modeled feed-forward contrast difference (ideal 1/4) from -0.0046, 0.0067 and -0.0018 to 0.0198, 0.0417 and 0.0135 (sampling error 0.0055 at 32,768 shots per arm); the ten-round circuit's order recovery stays at 0.129-0.139 in both forms, at or below the depolarized 0.1445 against the noiseless 0.334.
  Boundary: Statevector trajectories of live_run's calibrated noise channels on each circuit's active qubits under one retained snapshot per backend; the model omits crosstalk, leakage, drift and the scheduler's feed-forward residual, its shot counts omit live_run's simulation allowance, and admission and any hardware run need a fresh live_run dry run and an approved case.
  Assumptions: calibration_snapshot.noise_model equals live_run's noise model channel for channel (checked at export).
  Evidence: [noise.json](../evidence/linear_conjugation/noise.json), [README.md](../evidence/linear_conjugation/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-338, core-379.
  Depends on: n21-linear-encoding-routed.
- **qi-tuna17-zero-divisor-visibility-floor** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). Placed off Tuna-17 q0, q5, q6, q7 and q9 on regions {1,2,4,8,12} and {10,11,13,14,16}, three batches of the exact Clifford+T Design B witness of (e1 + e10)(e4 - e15) = 0 read P(s = 0) = 0.4854 [0.4800, 0.4908] dark, 0.5056 [0.5002, 0.5110] bright and 0.4918 fixture at 16384 shots per circuit, an endpoint contrast of 0.0202 +- 0.0039 (z = 5.2; readout-corrected bootstrap 0.022, 95 % 0.014 to 0.031), half the 0.042 of the three-region placement through the excluded qubits; by region the contrast is 0.0504 +- 0.0055 on {1,2,4,8,12} at 75 routed CZ and -0.0100 +- 0.0055 on {10,11,13,14,16} at 87 routed CZ, while every batch's readout-0 P1 stays at most 0.053 and readout-1 P1 at least 0.901 on every used qubit.
  Boundary: One 16384-shot run per circuit, the three batches minutes apart on the calibration after the platform's benchmarking, so drift between batches enters the case contrasts; the exclusion forced longer routes (75 to 91 CZ against 66 to 70 in the three-region placement), so the comparison with the earlier placement changes depth and qubits together and does not separate them; the per-region split rests on one replica per case and region; dark + bright - 1 = -0.009 (z = -2.3) and fixture - 1/2 = -0.008 (z = -3.0) repeat the earlier series' shift toward selector 1; the readout correction uses all-|0> and all-|1> circuits, which omit readout crosstalk under the witness's state.
  Evidence: [zero_divisor_tuna_17_visibility_floor_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/zero_divisor_tuna_17_visibility_floor_analysis.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-17T052547Z-zero_divisor_tuna_17-batch-00/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-17T052915Z-zero_divisor_tuna_17-batch-01/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-17T053226Z-zero_divisor_tuna_17-batch-02/results.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).

  Depends on: qi-tuna17-zero-divisor-contraction, qi-qx-zero-divisor-visibility-floor-twin.
- **qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 series P (targets Q0, Q4, Q5, Q6, Q10, Q11, Q12, Q16, 8192 shots per circuit) the preregistered rule reports 'not detected': 7 targets are usable (Q5 fails the 0.3 visibility floor), 3 classify as tails (Q6, Q10, Q16) and 2 as static offsets (Q11, Q12), below the 50% threshold. No classified target fits H2 (chi2 30.7 to 193.7 on 3 degrees of freedom); Q11's readout arms sit 19 to 33 kHz below its reference at every tau out to 2 us.
  Boundary: One series on one device state, read against its batch-00 calibration as the rule fixes; Q6's visibility of 1.08 shows that calibration no longer describes Q6 after the 04:12Z platform calibration, so its class rests on a stale correction. The rule compares H2 with H1 and tests neither model's fit, so the tail and offset classes mark arm-to-arm structure beyond shot noise rather than a residual-photon decay. P-05 and P-06 stay outside the analysis because they straddle the 04:04Z q9 change; the reruns replace them.
  Evidence: [readout_ringdown_tuna_17_P_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/readout_ringdown_tuna_17_P_analysis.json), [readout_ringdown_tuna_17_P_calibration_sensitivity.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/readout_ringdown_tuna_17_P_calibration_sensitivity.json), [readout_ringdown_tuna_17_null_calibration.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/readout_ringdown_tuna_17_null_calibration.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).

  Acquisitions: qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-00, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-01, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-02, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-03, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-04, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-05, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-05-rerun1, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-06, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-06-rerun1, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-07, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-batch-08, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-recal-041520, qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-P-recal-042425.
- **qi-tuna17-readout-ringdown-Q** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 series Q (targets Q1, Q2, Q3, Q7, Q8, Q9, Q13, Q14, Q15, 8192 shots per circuit; delays d = 2 and d = 77) the preregistered rule reports 'not detected': 7 targets are usable (Q7 and Q8 fail the 0.3 visibility floor), 3 classify as tails (Q9, Q14, Q15) and none as a static offset, below the 50% threshold; Q15's tail class holds under the all-shot estimator only, and its postselected estimate classifies as none. Q9 fits H2 (chi2 6.95 on 3 degrees of freedom) while Q14 (146.8) and Q15 (39.3) do not; Q14's readout arms sit 43 to 61 kHz below its reference from tau = 100 ns out to 2 us.
  Boundary: One series on one device state, read against its batch-00 calibration as the rule fixes; Q15's visibility of 1.28 to 1.46 shows that calibration no longer describes Q15, so its class rests on a stale correction. The rule compares H2 with H1 and tests neither model's fit, so a tail class marks arm-to-arm structure beyond shot noise rather than a residual-photon decay. The postselection check bounds the shift from discarding mid-circuit-outcome shots at |z| <= 4.2 (Q13).

- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-pre-t0-readout-recovered** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). In the pre-T0 batch on Tuna-17 (4096 shots per circuit, jobs finished 15:38Z) the Q0 and Q6 readout fault of the morning is absent: Q0 reads P(1|1) 0.973 and Q6 0.957 against the precondition limits 0.7 and 0.3, and Q9 reads P(1|0) 0.018, so the precondition reports fault-recovered and the double-X discriminator is not read. Every qubit of the admitted layout {1, 4, 8, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14} passes the T0 readout rule (readout-1 P1 0.912 to 0.975, readout-0 P1 at most 0.045), and q5, q6, q7 and q9 are readout-admissible; q5, q6 and q7 keep their Ramsey visibility exclusion.
  Boundary: One batch on one calibration, taken after the platform benchmarking the operator observed at 15:34Z; the readout state can change at the next calibration. The double-X P1 values it records (Q0 0.019, Q6 0.110) are not a discriminator reading, since the precondition failed, and the proposed discriminator claim proposed-tuna17-q0-q6-double-x-discriminator stays untested. Readout admission lifts no visibility exclusion.

- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-t0-depth-ladder** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 the reduced two-label LCU of edge (1,10)(4,15) of kite 3, corrected per batch with its own readout pair, keeps a contrast C(1) = 0.954 +- 0.003, C(5) = 0.857 +- 0.003 and C(9) = 0.710 +- 0.003 over a 3 x 3 Latin square of pairs {(1,4), (10,13), (12,14)} and batches at 16384 shots, so the per-CZ retention is r = 0.9654 with 99% bootstrap interval [0.9638, 0.9671], inside the decision band [0.87, 0.97] as preregistered and above its prediction [0.87, 0.96]. The depth-1 upper bound 0.961 clears the 0.5 floor, so the program continues; C(1) and r both miss their predictions ([0.72, 0.82] and [0.87, 0.96]) upward. The QX twins of the three witness circuits read every replica's predicted outcome in all 2048 shots.
  Boundary: One calibration, three pairs and one edge; the dark replica of each depth sits on one pair and batch, so C(n) carries that pair's offset, and a pair x depth interaction stays inside r. The upward miss of C(1) comes from the prediction, which carried the selector's raw readout factor (about 0.87) while C(n) is readout-corrected; 0.82 / 0.874 = 0.94. Every bright replica cleared the depth-1 bright floor.
  Evidence: [box_kite_tuna_17_pre_t0_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/box_kite_tuna_17_pre_t0_analysis.json), [experiment-program.md](../notes/box_kites/experiment-program.md), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-425; source regenerated by core-420.

- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-follow-crossover** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 the packed follow series (7 batches, 21 jobs at 16384 shots, readout brackets in batches 0, 3 and 6 that moved at most 0.0076) gives every preregistered decision the census predicts. The reduced two-label LCU classifies all six rows: the kite-3 edge D = -0.964, -0.953, -0.916 on (1,4), (10,13), (12,14), its kite-6 twist D = +0.967, +0.952, the strut D = 0.001 (SE 0.002 to 0.004). Twist equality holds in all four block x setting crossovers at shot noise without a pair allowance: |Delta| at most 0.0040, |z| at most 2.44 against 3. Every depth-1 X conditional coherence has the census sign (E_0 = -1 bright, E_1 = +1 dark), and all eight strut branch expectations lie inside 0.1 + 3 sigma. Both associator walks read <X_c> with the sign of omega = -1 (-0.784 +- 0.006 on star 14 {11, 12, 16}, 6 CZ; -0.937 +- 0.006 on path 4 {1, 2}, 4 CZ), their omit-last controls stay inside 0.1 + 3 sigma, and the walk magnitudes 0.819 and 0.938 lie inside 0.10 of T0's r^n_CZ (0.810, 0.869). The five QX twin batches read every deterministic replica's outcome in all 2048 shots.
  Boundary: One calibration of one evening (23:08Z to 23:29Z), three pairs, one edge, its twist and one strut, and two walk exemplars. The twist test differences replicas of one batch, so drift between batches cancels; the classification and coherence signs are deterministic-limit decisions that the batch-to-batch scatter of the exploratory claim does not reach. The cross-predicted magnitudes are reported, not decided: two depth-1 X coherences on (10,13) read |E| = 1.10 after readout correction, outside the 0.954 +- 0.10 band, because the bracket's e10 for q13 exceeds its witness-context value (claim qi-tuna17-boxkite-follow-ladder-drift).
  Evidence: [box_kite_tuna_17_follow_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/box_kite_tuna_17_follow_analysis.json), [experiment-program.md](../notes/box_kites/experiment-program.md), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).

- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-ladder-readout-context** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 the |1> misassignment of q13 depends on the preparation context of the chip: e10 reads 0.0895 and 0.0863 with all 17 qubits excited (readout-1, batches 0 and 6) against 0.0441 and 0.0399 with q1, q13 and q14 alone excited and every coupled neighbor in |0> (readout-iso-x), differences 0.045 and 0.046 at about 45 standard errors, so context is established on q13 by the preregistered rule (above 0.02 and 5 SE in both brackets). It is absent on q1 (-0.0009, -0.0016) and q14 (-0.0027, -0.0006). The preparation path is absent under the 0.02 threshold on all three: e10 after H Z H minus the interpolated isolated e10 is +0.016 (q13), +0.005 (q14) and -0.0002 (q1). Corrected with the readout-1 brackets, the depth-1 X conditional coherence on (10,13) reads -1.104 and -1.060 (batches 1 and 4); corrected with q13's isolated e10 it reads -0.997 and -0.955, within one of the physical bound; (12,14) reads -0.995 and -0.994 with readout-1 and -0.999 and -0.995 with q14's isolated e10. The follow series' unphysical -1.105 on (10,13) is therefore a calibration artifact of the all-qubit readout-1 bracket applied to q13.
  Boundary: One device state of one night (01:55Z to 03:02Z, 2026-10-18) at 131072 shots per job. readout-1 and readout-iso-x differ both in the neighbors' state at measurement and in the X pulses applied to the neighbors, so the design does not separate state-dependent readout of q13 from drive crosstalk of those pulses on q13's preparation; readout-1 also excites q10 and q11 together, so it does not say which neighbor matters. The preparation differences below the 0.02 threshold are significant on shot noise (q13 +0.016 at about 20 SE, q14 +0.005 at about 9 SE) and are reported, not decided. Beside an excited q13, q10's own e01 rises by 0.011 to 0.014 against readout-0 (reported).
  Evidence: [box_kite_tuna_17_ladder_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/box_kite_tuna_17_ladder_analysis.json), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-425; source regenerated by core-420.
  Acquisitions: qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-00, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-01, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-02, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-03, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-04, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-05, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-06, qi-boxkite-qx-ladder-twins-batch-00, qi-boxkite-qx-ladder-twins-batch-01.
- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-ladder-within-batch** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). The within-batch Latin-square ladder on Tuna-17 (every circuit holding CZ depths 1, 5 and 9 on the pairs (1,4), (10,13) and (12,14); X batches 1 and 4, Y batches 2 and 5; 131072 shots per job) gives rho = r_coh / r_pop above 1 on all three pairs in both X batches: 1.021 and 1.020 on (1,4), 1.021 and 1.022 on (10,13), 1.005 and 1.006 on (12,14), each 99% interval excluding 1 (preregistered decision: above 1). The replicate difference exceeds 3 combined standard errors on (12,14) only (z -3.85; (1,4) 1.59, (10,13) -2.68). r_pop is 0.951 to 0.957 on (1,4), 0.953 to 0.958 on (10,13) and 0.976 to 0.978 on (12,14) across all four Latin batches. The 50 Hz line is detected in all 12 Latin jobs, as predicted; the depth-9 over depth-1 amplitude margin holds in 10 of 12 jobs and is refuted in the two jobs whose depth-9 pair is (12,14) in batches 4 and 5 (batch-04 latin-x-1 and batch-05 latin-y-1), where that selector's first harmonic falls to 0.0027.
  Boundary: rho above 1 means the population contrast decays faster than the joint coherence, which neither a global depolarizing channel (rho = 1) nor dephasing (rho below 1) gives; a coherent error that moves selector population, or the 50 Hz modulation averaged over a job, would raise it, and the design isolates neither. The preregistered rho uses the readout-1 correction, which overstates q13's e10 (claim qi-tuna17-boxkite-ladder-readout-context); with every iso qubit's e10 from readout-iso-x, rho reads 1.021 and 1.020 on (1,4), 1.020 and 1.022 on (10,13), 1.005 and 1.006 on (12,14), each 99% interval again excluding 1, so the decision is the same under both corrections. The line refutation is of the preregistered first-harmonic margin; the second harmonic of the same selector stays at 0.020 to 0.022 (claim qi-tuna17-boxkite-ladder-drift-harmonics), so the refutation does not show the modulation absent.
  Evidence: [box_kite_tuna_17_ladder_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/box_kite_tuna_17_ladder_analysis.json), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-425; source regenerated by core-420.
  Acquisitions: qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-00, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-01, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-02, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-03, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-04, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-05, qi-boxkite-tuna17-ladder-batch-06, qi-boxkite-qx-ladder-twins-batch-00, qi-boxkite-qx-ladder-twins-batch-01.
- **qd-belenos-bright-block-routing-deficit** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On qpu:belenos the bright block that sim:belenos reproduces exactly, sent as a 4-mode unitary inside a 24-mode identity and compiled to the same 552-beam-splitter circuit, keeps 0.470 (Wilson 0.460 to 0.480) of one-click samples on its four modes, and of those an algebra share a = 0.256 (0.244 to 0.269) against the ideal 1; the leaked clicks fall mostly on modes 14 to 19, whose dark counts are below 0.2 per second.
  Boundary: One 10000-sample job at the 2026-10-17 calibration (HOM 92.6 %, g2 1 %). The root-cause record (docs/evidence/quandela/belenos_leakage_rca.json) refutes a circuit, encoding, convention or multi-photon cause: the decoded compiled mesh reproduces the ideal, and no single-parameter error model reaches the shot-noise floor; the exact replay after a calibration reproduces the leak, and 64 random meshes of the same session show errors about a fifth the size the leak needs, so the leak stays specific to this job's form or mesh.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-17T213646Z-qpu_belenos-box_kite_photonic_single-job-04/analysis.json), [belenos_leakage_rca.json](../evidence/quandela/belenos_leakage_rca.json), [strategy.md](../notes/quandela/strategy.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-508, core-511; source regenerated by core-504.
  Acquisitions: qd-qpu-belenos-single-job-04.
  Depends on: qd-sim-belenos-boxkite-ideals.

On qpu:belenos the exact Q1 bright-block body, re-run after the calibration that moved the HOM figure from 92.6 % to 57.6 % with unchanged couplers, compiled to the identical circuit (sha256 9bbbaf63) and kept 0.504 (Wilson 0.494 to 0.514) of one-click samples on the block's four modes against Q1's 0.470 (0.460 to 0.480), with the leaked light again mostly on modes 17, 18 and 19; the HOM figure does not index this one-photon route's leakage.

- **qd-belenos-random-meshes-no-class-fits-under-receipt-model** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On qpu:belenos four iterated jobs of 16 seeded uniform-random meshes each (64 settings of 1500 samples, 95,988 of the 96,000 one-click, one calibration session, all re-transpiled) read 'no class fits' under the preregistered analysis: no scanned heater-noise, gain, crosstalk or misroute magnitude reaches the fit test (median TVD 9.6 to 15.4 shot-noise floors per job), and shared static heater offsets are not declared (held-out gain about -79000 nats against fitted dephasing at the best prior width, median held-out TVD 20 floors).
  Boundary: The preregistered instrument model weights each mode by the receipt's t_out and subtracts the receipt's dark rates; the runs refute both (t_out reads 3 to 6 % on modes 0 to 11 and 194 to 400 % on modes 12 to 23, while modes 0 to 11 collect about twice the clicks of 12 to 23), so the verdict holds for that model and does not name the chip's optical error class.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T030133Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-03/analysis.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T030428Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-04/analysis.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T030731Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-05/analysis.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T031025Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-06/analysis.json), [static.json](../../results/quandela/sessions/2026-10-18T030133Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh/static.json), [strategy.md](../notes/quandela/strategy.md).

- **qi-tuna17-q13-readout-context-q11** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 the |1> misassignment of q13 follows the state of q11 alone. With q13 alone excited (readout-q13) e10 reads 0.0456 (SE 0.0006); with q11 also excited (readout-q13-q11) 0.0913, a difference of +0.0457 at about 21 SE once q13's bracket drift between batch-00 and batch-03 (0.0019) joins the shot-noise SE in quadrature (about 45 SE on shot noise alone), established by the preregistered rule (at least 0.02 and 3 SE); with q10 excited (readout-q13-q10) 0.0396, -0.0060, and after X X on q10 and q11 that leaves both in |0> (readout-q13-xx) 0.0419, -0.0037, neither established. readout-1, with all 17 qubits excited, reads 0.0898, the q11 value. The preregistered attribution is 'state': the context dependence of q13 that the ladder established follows q11 ending in |1>. The drive control gives each neighbor two X pulses and returns it to |0>, and q10's single X raises nothing, so neither a drive effect that grows with the pulse count nor q10's single pulse explains the rise; a transient of q11's single pulse that a second pulse cancels is not separated from q11's state.
  Boundary: One device state (17:00Z to 17:31Z, 2026-10-18), one excitation per context circuit at 131072 shots, q13 only. The q10 and drive differences are significant on shot noise alone (about 7.6 and 4.5 SE, 2.9 and 1.8 with the drift term) and below the 0.02 threshold, so they are reported, not decided. No control matches the q11 arm's single pulse with q11 returned to |0>, so a pulse-history effect of that one pulse is not excluded; the mechanism of the q11 dependence (a dispersive shift, readout-resonator crosstalk or residual coupling at measurement) is not identified, and the other block qubits' contexts were not measured.
  Evidence: [walsh_exemplar_tuna_17_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/walsh_exemplar_tuna_17_analysis.json), [tuna17-walsh-exemplars.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna17-walsh-exemplars.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-557.
  Acquisitions: qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-00, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-01, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-02, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-03.
  Depends on: qi-tuna17-boxkite-ladder-readout-context.
- **qi-tuna17-walsh-exemplar-classes** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). The cubic Walsh exemplars (1,16) (K = 11/32, a 5-CZ circuit on three of a block's four qubits) and (9,18) (K = 5/32, 6 CZ on all four) ran on Tuna-17 blocks X = q1-q4-q8-q12 and Y = q10-q13-q11-q14 in both assignments, twice each. The preregistered decisions: the class order K_high > K_low holds on each block in both batches, the block's high-class job against its low-class job, at z 105 to 114 with the bracket drift in the SE; readout-corrected K (q13 by its neighbors' true state) reads 0.2896 and 0.2890 (high, X), 0.3050 and 0.3054 (high, Y), 0.1296 and 0.1313 (low, X), 0.1251 and 0.1252 (low, Y); the class-by-block interaction I = -0.148 (SE 0.010, a bootstrap that resamples each circuit's shots jointly over both blocks) is established, the high class doing better on Y and the low class on X; the depolarizing-shape G test rejects every job and block (log10 p -26 to -1066) under the bracket mean and under each single bracket. Replicates agree within a TVD of 0.004 to 0.008. The informational global-depolarizing prediction (0.2595 high, 0.1236 low) undershoots the high class.
  Boundary: One device state (17:00Z to 17:31Z, 2026-10-18). The high-class circuit leaves its block's fourth qubit idle, so its forbidden outcomes measure that idle qubit alone (weight 0.003 on X, below 0.0002 on Y) and the uniform-over-16 depolarizing null is misspecified for it: the high-class shape rejection carries no information, while its K below ideal with no forbidden weight shows the noise moving weight among the eight allowed outcomes. For the same reason the interaction does not isolate a class-specific coherent error: the low class alone uses the fourth qubit and the couplers q8-q12 and q11-q14, so the classes run on different hardware subsets. The low-class shape rejection holds under all three q13 corrections and is conditional on the per-qubit readout model. The circuits test a CCZ phase polynomial under Tuna-17 noise, not composition of the sedenion multiplication blocks.
  Evidence: [walsh_exemplar_tuna_17_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/walsh_exemplar_tuna_17_analysis.json), [tuna17-walsh-exemplars.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna17-walsh-exemplars.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-557; replay failure paths checked by core-432.
  Acquisitions: qi-walsh-exemplar-qx-twins-batch-00, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-00, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-01, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-02, qi-walsh-exemplar-tuna17-batch-03.

- **qd-belenos-q1-failure-persists-until-mesh-load** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). In one qpu:belenos job (46b810ae, case belenos_mesh/job-16, 24 entries of Q1's circuit at 10000 samples, 149 s) the two leading seed-20 entries on 29dc6ef8 failed (tvd_cone_from_ideal 0.819 and 0.814; blocks (1708, 7, 1378, 1175) and (1788, 25, 1427, 1215) on modes 20 to 23), reproducing job-14's histogram within TVD 0.04 to 0.05, so a second entry on the same mesh does not recover; every one of the 11 following 14a96ca6 entries passed (0.080 to 0.103) and every one of the 11 29dc6ef8 entries that each followed a 14a96ca6 entry passed (0.078 to 0.104, post-load failure share 0 of 11, Wilson 95 % 0 to 0.26). The wrong state set at the start of a job on 29dc6ef8 persists while the job keeps that mesh and clears after one load of another mesh; the preregistered extension reading is '29dc6ef8 recovers after a load of another mesh while repeating the job's first mesh kept the failure'.
  Boundary: One job, one target mesh and one reference mesh; entry order is assumed to be execution order since no result entry carries a timestamp. The failing state is reproducible within this session (job-14 and job-16 30 minutes apart) but differs from Q1's and job-13's earlier histograms, and its realized transformation matches no ideal output of the 135 meshes served to this account (best TVD 0.20) or any other source mode of 29dc6ef8 (best 0.41). Why a job that opens on 29dc6ef8 at 10000 samples fails while D2's 1500-sample opening entry on it passed, and whether a reference warm-up entry recovers other meshes or other jobs, stay open; the service's default DetectorBalancing applied throughout.
