- **hw-n21-orbit-window-overlap** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On ibm_fez the N=21 orbit loses no resolved contrast to a longer barrier-equalized feed-forward window: with the control on qubit 144 the window_delay_ramp contract fails (the doubled delay reads 0.008 above the single one), and on the pinned 083417 roles the bracketed window_delay arm reads 0.228 against the nominal 0.235. Dropping the window barriers reproduces the 083417 program and its loss, 0.094 against the bracketed 0.228 (window_overlap_loss passes, upper limit -0.110), so removing the window barriers, which lets work-qubit gates overlap the control's measure-and-reset span, costs about 59 percent of the contrast magnitude on that calibration.
  Boundary: The run establishes the overlap cost on its calibration and attributes it to no single overlapping gate (the unbracketed arm is resynthesized, 43 CZ and 77 sx against 44 and 79); that the same overlap produced 083417's loss is the inference its identical program supports. The control-144 and control-142 assignments read different nominal magnitudes (0.151 and 0.235) under different calibrations.
  Evidence: [between-arm.json](../../results/live/2026-10-19T151959.226467Z/between-arm.json), [between-arm.json](../../results/live/2026-10-19T170620.832658Z/between-arm.json), [N21_ORBIT_RCA.md](../../results/live/N21_ORBIT_RCA.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-613; active code regression checked by core-612.
  Acquisitions: fez-n21-orbit-window-sweep, fez-n21-orbit-overlap-split.
- **hw-n21-spectator-143-beat** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). Qubit 143 of ibm_fez loses about 37 percent of its Ramsey visibility across the 836 dt (3.3 us) orbit window with control 142 held in |0> (0.622 against 0.989 at once), and a control-state-dependent coupling accounts for at most 0.042 of it (spectator_control_zz fails, idle 0.616 against idle_ground 0.622). The grounded delay sweep reads visibility 0.997, 0.612, 0.395, 0.885, 0.743, 0.255 and 0.705 at 0 to 20.064 us in 3.344 us steps: the preregistered decay_shape test reads neither, the visibilities are non-monotone, and the T2* of 7 us drawn from two points is withdrawn.
  Boundary: The preregistered shape and detuning fits describe no identified parameter (the stretch exponent sits on its bound and the single-frequency model is contradicted). The two-component fit (beat-model.json static model: a = 0.644, envelope 135.6 us) is descriptive and post hoc, and the non-monotone revivals it describes reject no monotone model through a registered decision; its splitting aliases at the 3.344 us sampling, and charge-parity splitting against a two-level-system coupling is undecided; a dense 0.25 us sweep resolves both.
  Evidence: [between-arm.json](../../results/live/2026-10-19T231043.083602Z/between-arm.json), [ramsey.json](../../results/live/2026-10-20T031212.175942Z/ramsey.json), [beat-model.json](../../results/live/2026-10-20T031212.175942Z/beat-model.json), [beat-model-9bd206ca42ea.json](../../results/live/2026-10-20T031212.175942Z/beat-model-9bd206ca42ea.json), [N21_ORBIT_RCA.md](../../results/live/N21_ORBIT_RCA.md), [N21_SPECTATOR_RAMSEY_CASE.md](../../results/live/N21_SPECTATOR_RAMSEY_CASE.md).
  Gates: active code regression checked by core-600, core-603, core-605.
  Acquisitions: fez-n21-spectator-ground-control, fez-n21-spectator-ramsey-sweep.
- **qi-qx-bdsc-pauli-twin-control** (preregistered emulator control, hardware). The hosted QX emulator returned the preregistered exact outcomes of all 41 BDSC Pauli readout circuits of the twin series (14 batches, 2048 shots each, completed 2026-10-22 10:35 to 10:41 UTC, 0 credits): every kernel+bright-1 pair residual and middle or sector residual is within its preregistered bound, and the matched quadrature phase is 0.0039, 0.0029 and -0.0181 rad (standard error 0.0156) on the three patches, consistent with zero. The control verdict passes with no miss, so each Tuna-17 patch's gate sequence, which the case builder requires the twin to repeat on relabeled qubits, is semantically clean before it reaches Tuna-17.
  Boundary: Noiseless emulator semantics of the twin's 10-qubit circuits, which pack two relabeled patches each; the 17-qubit Tuna-17 files, their physical qubit indices and their three-patch composition are not executed by this control, and it carries no device noise, readout or calibration behavior. The retained analysis was written by the Quantum Inspire interpreter and is bound by sha256 in every Tuna-17 series-gate.json, so its replay accepts float rounding within 1e-12 relative.
  Evidence: [bdsc_pauli_qx_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/bdsc_pauli_qx_analysis.json), [cases.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/cases/bdsc_pauli_qx/cases.json), [bdsc-tuna17-pauli-pilot.md](../design/bdsc-tuna17-pauli-pilot.md), [bdsc-tuna17-pauli-pilot.md](../runbooks/bdsc-tuna17-pauli-pilot.md).

- **qi-tuna17-bdsc-pauli-pilot-drift-stop** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). The preregistered Tuna-17 pilot of the BDSC target effect (27 circuits in 9 batches of 3, 32768 shots each, all completed, 540.8 s of execution, 0 credits, created 2026-10-22 10:42 UTC and finished 11:49 UTC) ended with its bracket drift stop fired: the readout-1 reference's P(0|1) on qubit 1 moved from 0.0735 in batch 06 to 0.0453 in batch 08, a change of 0.0283 against the 0.02 stop. The batch-00 assignment stop stayed clear, and the drift between consecutive references stayed at or below 0.0187 through batch 07. Under the preregistered rule a fired stop withholds every score, pair chi-square, phase and mitigated estimate, so the series makes no endpoint decision.
  Boundary: The registered decision is the stop itself. The backend reported calibrating at 11:32 UTC, between batches 06 and 08, which fits a recalibration as the cause of the drift but is not established by any retained probe. No witness score, branch phase or device qualification follows from these acquisitions, and nothing here bears on any Belenos measurement.

- **iqm-garnet-reset-billing-pilot** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On IQM Garnet (station control 50.0.0, calibration set 9069c4ef), two jobs of X and a bare measure on QB1 at 2000 shots each billed ceil(runtime), not ceil(the compiler's execution runtime estimate): 2 s for a 1.306 s runtime under passive reset and 1 s for 0.205 s under active_reset_cycles 1, against estimates of 2.804 s and 1.617 s, 1.50 credits in all. The estimates follow 1.60 s per job plus each shot's schedule within 0.02 s. Execution took 316 us per shot under passive reset and 37.5 us under active reset. Active reset left the bare measure at P(1) 0.0680 against 0.0065 under passive reset (difference 0.0615, z 10.4), so one active-reset cycle does not keep QB1's initialization. The passive job read X at 0.9705 and the bare measure at 0.0065, within three standard errors of the calibration-built predictions 0.9817 and 0.0046 (z -2.96 and +1.06).
  Boundary: One qubit (QB1), one calibration set and one active-reset setting (one cycle); billing is the service's rule on 2026-10-22. The excess bare-measure P(1) under active reset is not separated into residual excitation and reset-induced readout error. The H4 bare-measure prediction in the design document was the simulator's 2000-shot sample 0.003; the decision uses the exact unheralded P(1|0) 0.0046. The garnet:mock jobs fix the estimate model only; their measurement data are random bits.

- **iqm-garnet-signed-null-retest** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On IQM Garnet (calibration set 68b0f1d0, the retained 199-CZ compile of the signed kernel probe, two +, -, -, + blocks at |t| = 0.25 and at t = 0, 20000 shots each, heralding keeping 50263 to 50591 shots per arm), the odd part (Z(t) - Z(-t))/2 of QB7 read +0.0090 +- 0.0031 at |t| = 0.25 and +0.0025 +- 0.0031 at t = 0 (copies weighted equally), so D(0.25) = odd(0.25) - odd(0) = +0.0065 +- 0.0045 (z 1.46): unresolved under the preregistered three-standard-error decision. The visibility after the null subtraction is 0.058, inside the calibration model's 0.05 to 0.09 and a quarter of the 0.206 the original run implied; the even part read -0.0175 and -0.0195, reproducing the original run's negative offsets. The jobs charged 5.50 credits.
  Boundary: One calibration set. The observable is kernel-independent (signed-overlap-kernel-independence), so the re-test bounds the device's carried phase, not the sedenion kernel. The original run's +0.022 at |t| = 0.25 lies 1.8 standard errors (their combined error) above this run's +0.0090 at the same |t|; with that run's +t before -t order and no t = 0 arm, a batch-position effect, the null offset and a fluctuation are not separated.

- **iqm-garnet-kernel-cancellation** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On IQM Garnet (calibration set 68b0f1d0, 37 CZ on QB6, QB7, QB11, QB12 and QB17, one active-reset cycle with all-zeros heralding), the kernel cancellation probe read the control's odd part (Z(phi) - Z(-phi))/2 at -0.0699 +- 0.0087 for phi = 0.1 (z -8.0) and -0.178 +- 0.009 for phi = 0.3 (z -20.1), resolving the zero-divisor sign of the certified sedenion kernel (the eigenvalue -1 of L_e1^T L_e10) at both angles, while the bright vector's odd part read -0.016 +- 0.009, within one standard error of its modeled -0.008 (H2 z -0.92). The sine-law contrast odd(0.3) - 2.84 odd(0.1) read +0.021 +- 0.026 (H3 z 0.78), the kernel even part +0.049 against its decomposition +0.046 (H4 z 0.33), and QB11's and QB12's prx trains fit relative over-rotations of +0.15 +- 0.07 % and -0.20 +- 0.06 % (H5 chi-square 5.1 and 1.1). No preregistered hypothesis is falsified. The visibility read 0.36 +- 0.05 against the model's 0.48, and the job charged 1.50 credits.
  Boundary: One compile, one calibration set and two angles. The kernel-minus-bright double difference, the test the Tuna-17 run showed is needed, reads -0.0538 +- 0.0124 (z -4.3) at phi = 0.1, and -0.132 +- 0.026 (z -5.0) at 0.3 if the bright artifact scales like sin(2 phi), since the case ran the bright arm at phi = 0.1 only. The prediction schedules idle relaxation ALAP; scheduled ASAP, IQM's pulse-builder default, it would move H4 to about z -2.5 and H2 to z -0.3 with no decision changed (issue #386). cd_core already settles the zero-divisor property exactly, so the run demonstrates that Garnet realizes the compiled sign table at first order; the bright control and the sine law bound circuit artifacts, and the even part's agreement with readout plus relaxation leaves no room for the large coherent error the signed probe's offsets once suggested.
  Evidence: [kernel_cancellation_probe_analysis-runner-image.json](../../results/iqm/kernel_cancellation_probe_analysis-runner-image.json), [kernel_cancellation_probe_analysis.json](../../results/iqm/kernel_cancellation_probe_analysis.json), [prediction.json](../../results/iqm/cases/kernel-cancellation-probe/prediction.json), [iqm-garnet-kernel-cancellation-probe.md](../design/iqm-garnet-kernel-cancellation-probe.md), [kernel_cancellation.py](../../gororoba_quantum/kernel_cancellation.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-641; source regenerated by core-642.
  Acquisitions: iqm-garnet-kernel-cancellation.
  Related claims: signed-overlap-kernel-independence.
  Supersedes: proposed-kernel-cancellation-probe.
- **qi-qx-kernel-cancellation-twin** (preregistered emulator control, hardware). On the QX emulator the 18-circuit twin of the Tuna-17 kernel cancellation series (six batches of three: the same compiled arms on register qubits 0-4, control q1, 2048 shots each) read the kernel odd parts -0.1848 +- 0.0109 at phi = pi/36 and -0.4944 +- 0.0096 at pi/12 against the ideal -0.1736 and -0.5000, within 1.03 and 0.58 standard errors, and the bright odd part exactly 0, so the platform executes the emitted cQASM as compiled.
  Boundary: An ideal emulator; it checks the emitted cQASM and the platform's handling of it, not device noise or the angle quantization of Tuna.

- **qi-tuna17-kernel-cancellation** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). On Tuna-17 (region 4, 8, 11, 12, 14, 45 CZ, every rotation on the pi/36 grid, 32768 shots per arm over two batches), the kernel cancellation probe read kernel odd parts of -0.0222 +- 0.0039 at phi = pi/36 and -0.0547 +- 0.0039 at pi/12, but the bright vector, whose ideal odd part is zero, read -0.0167 +- 0.0039 (z -4.3): the preregistered H2 is falsified and the probe carries a signed response that does not depend on the kernel. The preregistered H1, the kernel against zero, resolves at pi/36 and is withheld at pi/12, whose contrast ran out of order; with H2 falsified, that resolution does not establish the zero-divisor sign. The sine-law test is withheld, since the series measures no bright control at pi/12.
  Boundary: Six batches, each its own calibration context, pooled in pairs; one region and a bright arm at phi = pi/36 only, so the artifact at pi/12 is unmeasured. The platform runs each circuit as its own job in an order of its own: the provider start times show the pi/36 kernel and bright contrasts executed +, -, -, + as preregistered and the pi/12 kernel contrast +, -, +, -, a protocol deviation the analysis names. The bright response's mechanism (the non-unital relaxation that gives the bright arm a small odd part in the Garnet model, or a position effect that differs between a contrast's two batches) is untested on Tuna-17, which has no retained noise model.
  Assumptions: the two batches of a contrast carry the same within-batch position effect, so the +, -, -, + balance across them cancels it; a batch's readout assignment factor equals its batch pair's, whose two references sit in different batches; the spread of the series' readout references enters the corrected errors.
  Evidence: [kernel_cancellation_tuna_17_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/kernel_cancellation_tuna_17_analysis.json), [qi_cancellation_cases.py](../../scripts/qi_cancellation_cases.py), [algebra-hardware-probe-principles.md](../notes/topics/algebra-hardware-probe-principles.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-645; source regenerated by core-646.

- **qd-belenos-bdsc-stationarity-readout** (preregistered hardware inference, hardware). The preregistered stationarity case on qpu:belenos, four post-calibration jobs repeating the crossed BDSC diagnostic's frozen requests at 100000 samples per iteration with every returned circuit equal to its retained counterpart, reads its primary inconclusive under the frozen mode-5 detector_absent veto: the heterogeneity of matched block changes about their class means is 66.1 on 45 degrees of freedom (p 0.022; the subtract veto reads 65.0, p 0.027). The dark-click coincidence falsifier reads consistent (p 0.31), the superposition common shift reads +0.0003 (z 0.8) rather than repeating the retained -0.0044, and the superpositions keep an excess spread tau 0.0021 (interval 0.0009 to 0.0035) while f and g read none; the veto removes 0.999 of the superpositions' raw tau^2. Reported only: the mode-5 share fell from a block mean of 0.249 to 0.057 at a constant plan-time dark-count figure of 48440.3 counts/s, and the pre-calibration run of the b1-j1 requests against its repeat reads a superposition shift of -0.0017 (z -2.8) across the calibration.
  Boundary: One device and one calibration, two blocks about an hour apart; the case separates neither slow drift from a step change nor drift within a window. Standard errors and p-values are reference values under independent Bernoulli trials, uncalibrated while the provider contract leaves count provenance unresolved. The calibration hold and the post-calibration repeat of b1-j1 were amendments committed before the jobs they govern; the pre-calibration b1-j1 run enters no frozen test. The plan-time dark-count figure is a platform reading, not an acquisition-time measurement, so the mode-5 mechanism stays a candidate. An inconclusive primary decides neither branch; it makes no certification decision.
  Evidence: [bdsc_stationarity_calibration_contrast_pinned.json](../../results/quandela/bdsc_stationarity_calibration_contrast_pinned.json), [bdsc_stationarity_readout_pinned.json](../../results/quandela/bdsc_stationarity_readout_pinned.json), [bdsc-qualification.md](../design/bdsc-qualification.md), [bdsc-qualification-case.md](../runbooks/bdsc-qualification-case.md), [bdsc_stationarity.py](../../scripts/bdsc_stationarity.py).
  Gates: replayed by core-675.
  Acquisitions: qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-stationarity-b1-j1-precalibration, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-stationarity-b1-j1, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-stationarity-b1-j2, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-stationarity-b2-j1, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-stationarity-b2-j2.

## Exploratory claims

- **walk-scaffold-single-channel-exclusion** (exploratory reanalysis, implementation). Added to live_run's calibrated noise model under each member's execution calibration, none of six single candidate channels (end-of-circuit flips, SX over-rotation, CZ local phase, CZ conditional phase, extra amplitude damping, extra depolarizing; one parameter per qubit or edge, coherent angles within 0.15 rad) fitted on the walk readout0 member reproduces the floor and predicts the lane's 13 other walk members in any of the four Fez and Kingston lanes: held-out deviance 2,955 to 31,661 on 195 degrees of freedom. Three fits pass the readout0 gate: CZ local phase on Fez 1-4 (p = 0.018) and on Kingston 59, 73-75 (p = 0.30), and end flips on Kingston 59, 73-75 (p = 0.028); the two Kingston fits predict its held-out members worse than the baseline, and the Fez fit leaves held-out deviance 7,202.
  Boundary: A reanalysis of retained hardware counts seen before the candidates were chosen; it excludes six single-mechanism channels with one parameter per qubit or edge under the stated bounds, which nine of twelve coherent fits reach; combinations, spectator crosstalk and drifting errors are outside it, end-of-circuit flips do not separate a readout fault from leakage, and no device mechanism is identified.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/walk_scaffold_channels/result.json), [members.json](../evidence/walk_scaffold_channels/members.json), [members.qpy](../evidence/walk_scaffold_channels/members.qpy), [20261014T1947440700.json](../evidence/calibration/history/ibm_kingston/20261014T1947440700.json), [20261014T2025340700.json](../evidence/calibration/history/ibm_kingston/20261014T2025340700.json), [20261012T1920460700.json](../evidence/calibration/history/ibm_fez/20261012T1920460700.json), [collect.json](../../results/live/2026-10-13T025444.603480Z/collect.json), [collect.json](../../results/live/2026-10-15T031229.371738Z/collect.json), [README.md](../evidence/walk_scaffold_channels/README.md), [README.md](../evidence/walk-control-scaffolding/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-311.
  Acquisitions: fez-ripple-atlas, kingston-ripple-atlas.
  Depends on: hw-walk-scaffold-floor.
- **qd-bdsc-qualification-pipeline-runs-on-sim-belenos** (exploratory reanalysis, implementation). The frozen BDSC qualification job tc-*-e1 (12 iterations of the 77-parameter ladder-union circuit, 300000 samples each, QRNG seeds) ran end to end on sim:belenos under both policies: the empty-list job 19f18f8c in 148 s and the provider-default rerun cac942bd in 153 s, 1 credit each. Every iteration returned its 300000 samples with its circuit_params bit-exact and its compilation_seed echoed; f read 0 and g read 1 under both templates, the eight superposition states read 0.4989 to 0.5017 against 0.5, and the one-click rule excluded at most 21 samples per iteration. The first provider-default job 03dd195b stopped at iteration 4 (55 s of compute, progress 0.2567, progress message probs) and ended in Worker timeout after about 30 minutes without charge; the identical rerun completed.
  Boundary: Simulator runs only; sim:belenos applies a fixed noise model and its default DetectorBalancing returns counts drawn from a corrected distribution, so neither policy here tests the QPU's observation contract, and the readout admits qpu:belenos runs only. One timeout in two provider-default runs establishes no rate; Perceval computes the same noisy distribution in 0.1 s locally, so the stall is placed in the service worker by inference.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T063940Z-sim_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-none-e1/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T063940Z-sim_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-def-e1/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T071011Z-sim_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-def-e1/results.json), [bdsc-qualification-case.md](../runbooks/bdsc-qualification-case.md).
  Acquisitions: qd-sim-belenos-bdsc-tc-none-e1, qd-sim-belenos-bdsc-tc-def-e1-timeout, qd-sim-belenos-bdsc-tc-def-e1-rerun.
- **ascella-signed-coherence-device-diagnostics** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). In both Ascella signed-coherence runs the returned compiled circuits reproduce every registered statistic within 2.2e-4, while the block-conditioned X and Y statistics read 0.967 to 0.972 times their ideals and the direct readout 1.004 times; X residuals exceed multinomial noise (chi-square 230 and 493 on 18 degrees of freedom) more than Y (45 and 114). Outside-block clicks concentrate on modes 8 to 10, the detectors with the largest plan-time dark rates, with an excess on modes 4 and 6 (composition chi-square 110 and 211 on 7 degrees of freedom), and physical_perf reads 0.0209 in every iteration of different outside-block counts.
  Boundary: Descriptive fits without preregistered thresholds; the binomial errors ignore drift within an iteration. The dark-rate expectation uses plan-time rates, not acquisition-time ones, and a coincidence-window or gating model of the clicks is not established.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T091642Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-00/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-24T153028Z-qpu_ascella-ascella_signed_coherence-job-01/results.json), [job-00-diagnostics.json](../evidence/ascella-order-coefficients/job-00-diagnostics.json), [job-01-diagnostics.json](../evidence/ascella-order-coefficients/job-01-diagnostics.json), [ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.md](../design/ascella-signed-coherence-conformance.md), [literature.md](../notes/quandela/literature.md).

The explicitly authorized unchanged Ascella order pilot completed eight settings and returned 65536 detected samples in 25 execution seconds, with a token-reported debit of 1.016384 free credits. Conditional analysis accepts 65523 occupation-sum-one observations and retains 13 multiple-occupation exclusions. Measured-versus-requested total variation spans 0.00048828125 to 0.02796092796; returned compiled-versus-requested total variation stays below 0.000298160.

- **belenos-operator-repeat-counts-and-compilation-boundary** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The approved five-job Belenos programme completed 120 settings with 983040 returned samples in 724 execution seconds, charged 5.081920 credits. The descriptive reported-single-photon readout excludes and retains 350 outcomes whose total reported occupation is zero or greater than one. The 120 matched-repeat pair comparisons have maximum conditional total variation 0.036181 and identical returned compiled-circuit bytes within every matched pair. Returned compiled intensity distributions differ from requested ideal distributions by at most 0.000210 total variation. The imaginary-superposition Fourier control on representative zero instead gives measured-versus-compiled total variation from 0.996336 to 0.997313 across its three repeats, while its measured-versus-preceding-compiled distance is 0.030785 to 0.034049.
  Boundary: Conditional frequencies given reported total occupation one, and decoded numerical metadata from these five jobs. The historical JSON's one-click label denotes that occupation-sum selection; the mixed threshold/quasi-PNR documentation does not establish a uniform physical-click model. The records establish a compilation-metadata/measurement mismatch, rather than which applied-control or result-association mechanism caused it. Fixed request ordering confounds a setting-specific response with a preceding-setting effect. The analysis supplies neither calibrated uncertainty coverage, absolute transmission, process tomography nor fundamental quaternionic-physics evidence. A newly ordered intervention requires its own approved case.
  Evidence: [belenos-repeat-readout.json](../../results/hypercomplex/belenos-repeat-readout.json), [belenos-compilation-measurement-audit.json](../../results/hypercomplex/belenos-compilation-measurement-audit.json), [belenos-repeat-readout-pooled.json](../../results/hypercomplex/belenos-repeat-readout-pooled.json), [belenos-compilation-measurement-audit-pooled.json](../../results/hypercomplex/belenos-compilation-measurement-audit-pooled.json), [ascella-instrument-readiness-and-packed-tests.md](../design/ascella-instrument-readiness-and-packed-tests.md).

A native-mesh readiness recheck after an observed Ascella calibration-to-available status transition and changed published performance figures again returned the missing hardware_info.circuit_input_ports instrument error after one execution second, with zero shots, zero samples and zero reported credit debit.
  Boundary: One descriptive recheck submitted after the static readiness stopping rule had fired, without a recorded explicit override. The stopping-rule adjudication classifies this submission as a protocol deviation, rather than preregistered adherence. The observed public status transition was insufficient to restore this sampling path. The instrument's private configuration and internal worker control flow remain unobserved.

- **ascella-input-port-failure-across-payloads** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The eight-setting order iterator, static twelve-mode H coupler and static twelve-mode calibrated native mesh each failed after one execution second with zero shots and zero samples and the same missing hardware_info.circuit_input_ports diagnostic. Their collected token-usage deltas report zero credit debit. The failures span generic iterated, generic static and architecture-compatible static requests on connected input zero.
  Boundary: Three sampling requests under their recorded platform snapshots. The native-mesh request followed the static readiness stopping rule without a recorded explicit override; the stopping-rule adjudication classifies it as a protocol deviation. Its result is descriptive rather than evidence of preregistered adherence. The observations reject static payload class and native-architecture compatibility as sufficient remedies on the tested paths. They provide neither measured operator probabilities nor an exhaustive statement about every command or future configuration. The deployment's instrument map and private worker implementation remain unobserved.

- **hw-n21-orbit-phase** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). A one-phase work-superposition model fits the N=21 orbit runs (deviance 65.4 to 2.5 on the first run, phase +0.59 [0.52, 0.68] rad); mid-circuit assignment, reset failure, window T1 and window dephasing do not, and the echo arm lowers the fitted phase to +0.17 rad.

Descriptively the idle rotates C by -1.130 rad over 4.4 us of added exposure (|C| 0.413 to 0.375), and the measure-and-reset context adds +0.054 [0.002, 0.105] rad, inside the up-to-0.17 rad the unsigned negative control's bias allows.

The recorded first bit on qubit 79 reads 0 in about 11 percent of correction shots, and in the known-label arms terminal success falls from 0.80 to 0.54-0.57 in that stratum, which excludes independent, state-preserving assignment error.
  Boundary: A state change of qubit 79 and a common cause behind both the misread and the terminal loss remain; the strata stay descriptive.

On 2026-10-11 the pilot's hybrid Shor replica 0 read its qubit-0 output bit as 1 with probability 0.618 against 0.448-0.491 on five replicas with the same ideal law, which the corner flips measured on 2026-10-15 predict as 0.622: the qubit-0 readout fault persisted at least four days through recalibrations that never reported it.

- **hw-idle-detuning-scale** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The Kingston coherence-window rotation is a 40.9 kHz detuning difference between work qubits 68 and 66 (up to 227.7 kHz windings), the Fez N21 orbit phase is of the same order, and fringe phases spread -0.26 to +0.50 rad across tiles: a recurring scale of tens of kHz that backend properties do not report.

- **hw-atlas-frame-detuning** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). On the executed Fez ripple atlas circuits and IBM's executed pulse times, a static frame detuning per qubit, resimulated at its leave-one-out fitted values, predicts held-out edge fringe phases better than the lane mean on six of seven lanes (0.086 to 0.120 rad against 0.129 to 0.238 rad); the fitted detunings are mostly 5 to 30 kHz, up to about 60 kHz.
  Boundary: Inferred from one run's fringe phases; the detuning model's linear held-out residuals stay 1.8 to 3.0 times its leave-one-out noise floor, lane 0 is unexplained, and only a direct measurement such as conditional Ramsey confirms per-qubit detunings.
  Evidence: [measured-ripple.json](../../results/ripple/measured-ripple.json), [result.json](../evidence/atlas_detuning_map/result.json), [README.md](../evidence/atlas_detuning_map/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-285.
  Acquisitions: fez-ripple-atlas.
- **hw-atlas-coherent-families** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). On the executed Fez and Kingston ripple atlas circuits at IBM's executed pulse times, six coherent error families (frame detuning, CZ conditional phase, static ZZ in the partner-in-0 and symmetric frames, SX amplitude and axis tilt) fitted one at a time to the edge fringe phase offsets of all 14 lanes leave every lane except two (Fez lane 5 with axis tilt, Kingston lane 2 with detuning and with partner-in-0 ZZ) above 1.5 times its leave-one-out noise floor under every single family, the lowest ratio per lane ranging from 1.68 to 3.70, so no single family accounts for the offsets; partner-in-0 ZZ scores below the symmetric frame on 13 of 14 lanes in the linear fit and on all 14 resimulated, by a median factor of 2.4, and is the best single family on 7 lanes (detuning on 4, axis tilt on 3), while SX amplitude beats the lane mean on none.
  Boundary: Inferred from one run per backend; the best single family per lane is an after-the-fact selection among six, and the lane-4 ranking of the evidence README selects among eighteen models, so no family is confirmed, and a confirming test scores one named model on lanes it was not selected on. Each Kingston lane excludes two edges that span a calibration identity change. The partner-in-0 frame is physical if IBM calibrates each qubit frequency with its neighbors in |0>, which no repository source states; conditional Ramsey with the partner in |0> (proposed-conditional-ramsey-hamiltonian) measures the frame directly. On lane 4 the edge-to-edge contrast deficit stays at 2 to 2.5 times its floor under every phase-fitted model.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/atlas_coherent_families/result.json), [measured-ripple.json](../../results/ripple/measured-ripple.json), [README.md](../evidence/atlas_coherent_families/README.md), [coherent-error-families-report.md](../notes/topics/coherent-error-families-report.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-329, core-661.
  Acquisitions: fez-ripple-atlas, kingston-ripple-atlas.
  Depends on: hw-atlas-frame-detuning.
- **hw-ghost-calibration-replay** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Replaying the executed circuits of 14 retained IBM runs under every retained calibration of their backend, at each member's executed shot count, the noise model reproduces the recorded replay distances to sampling precision (mean |ghost - recorded| 0.0077 TV over 206 replicas against 0.0079 TV between two independent ghost draws), the predicted distance to hardware moves by only 0.001 to 0.032 TV across calibrations against floors of 0.026 to 0.435 TV, and today's calibration fits the old data better than the execution-time calibration by more than two standard errors on 10 of 14 runs. Five 2026-10-18 Kingston runs and one further Kingston snapshot, added by an incremental merge, widen the calibration spread to as much as 0.054 TV (2026-10-12T055624, 0.088 to 0.142), and the exact-law n21 members and the 149-member control runs reproduce their recorded replays within 0.0027 TV. A second merge adds ten 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-20 runs and six snapshots: the four init-control runs whose every job ended in ERROR are skipped, and the six that returned shots reproduce their recorded replays within 0.0056 TV, their predicted distance moving by at most 0.008 TV across calibrations. A third merge adds the two 2026-10-21 segmented sweeps and two snapshots: both reproduce their recorded replays within 0.011 TV, and their distances to hardware (0.509 and 0.451 TV) stay near the one-line model's limit, which no readout factor closes (part_f).
  Boundary: Calibration identity explains little of the mismatch; whether the model underestimates noise is not settled (today's CZ error is higher on 9 of the 13 kernel runs that play CZ, while readout changes correlate with the opposite sign), and an error-scale sweep per run is the test.
  Evidence: [result.json](../evidence/ghost_runs/result.json), [report.json](../evidence/ghost_runs/report.json), [qpu-job-retention-audit.json](../../results/live/qpu-job-retention-audit.json), [collect.json](../../results/kaggle/2026-10-16T090343Z-gororoba-ghost-runs/collect.json), [README.md](../evidence/ghost_runs/README.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-286, core-287.
  Acquisitions: kingston-mod15-pilot, kingston-folded-qpe-ladder, kingston-correction-first, kingston-correction-second, fez-ripple-atlas, fez-rank-sectors, fez-n21-orbit-feedback, fez-n21-orbit-window, fez-patch-window, kingston-coherence-window, kingston-rank-sectors, kingston-ripple-atlas, kingston-corner-baseline, kingston-zero-divisor-fringe, kingston-init-control-prep0-50us, kingston-init-control-xhistory-250us, kingston-init-control-xhistory-50us, kingston-init-control-prep0-250us, kingston-n21-feedback-encoded-50us, kingston-init-control-xhistory-20us, kingston-init-control-prep0-20us, fez-n21-orbit-window-sweep, fez-n21-orbit-overlap-split, fez-n21-spectator-ground-control, fez-n21-spectator-ramsey-sweep, marrakesh-n21-q47-segmented-baseline, fez-n21-q143-segmented-sweep.
- **qi-tuna17-neighbor-drive-phase** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Driving a Tuna-17 neighbor with 40 X gates inside an 800 ns target idle shifts the target's idle phase by up to 0.633 rad (Q7 on Q4) and 0.695 rad under simultaneous drive (Q7 and Q8 on Q4), against undriven idle phases of -0.03 to -0.12 rad; the pairwise residual phi(AB) - phi(A) - phi(B) + phi(none) departs from zero beyond |z| = 3.29 on targets 3 (z = -3.38) and 11 (z = -3.43) and stays within |z| < 2.1 on targets 0, 4 and 12, and conditioning a target on its driver's final outcome leaves its phase within a few hundredths of a radian. The QX reference reads residuals of 0.012 to 0.077 rad against a 0.044 rad sigma.
  Boundary: The circuits, shots and qubit roles were fixed in cases.json before submission, while the analysis (phase-domain residual, driver and conditioning fields, every threshold) was fixed after the data arrived; the batch's five jobs (four additivity configurations, then teleport) ran sequentially about 11 s apart, so drift between jobs is not cancelled and enters every cross-job difference; each star and each path occupies its own qubits, so every value is a per-star or per-path input and forms no device-wide average; the counts carry no state-preparation or readout correction; one 16384-shot run on one Tuna-17 calibration. A ZZ phase from a driver's time-averaged excitation during its X train is not separated from a drive-induced Stark shift.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/analysis.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/results.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-QXsinglenodesimulator/analysis.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-331.
  Acquisitions: qi-tuna17-crosstalk-teleport, qi-qx-crosstalk-teleport-reference.
- **qi-tuna17-simultaneous-drive-degradation** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Driving Tuna-17 qubits Q1 and Q2, which share no coupler, with 40 X gates each at the same time raises Q2's P1 after the even X train from 0.046 (driven alone) to 0.893 (z = 153.6) and Q1's from 0.062 to 0.259 (z = 48.5); the other eight drivers change P1 by at most 0.025 under simultaneous drive.
  Boundary: The circuits, shots and qubit roles were fixed in cases.json before submission, while the analysis (phase-domain residual, driver and conditioning fields, every threshold) was fixed after the data arrived; the batch's five jobs (four additivity configurations, then teleport) ran sequentially about 11 s apart, so drift between jobs is not cancelled and enters every cross-job difference; each star and each path occupies its own qubits, so every value is a per-star or per-path input and forms no device-wide average; the counts carry no state-preparation or readout correction; one 16384-shot run on one Tuna-17 calibration. The design does not isolate the mechanism (drive-line crosstalk, a shared control resource or a classical scheduling effect); the 0.85 change on Q2 exceeds the drift any other driver shows, which does not exclude drift on Q2 alone.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/analysis.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/results.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-331.
  Acquisitions: qi-tuna17-crosstalk-teleport.
- **qi-tuna17-driver-overrotation** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Driven alone, Tuna-17 drivers Q6, Q8 and Q9 read P1 = 0.717, 0.764 and 0.883 after 40 X gates (ideal 0), above the 1/2 that depolarizing, relaxation and readout error reach from |0>; a pure per-gate over-rotation needs at least 0.050, 0.053 and 0.061 rad per X.
  Boundary: The circuits, shots and qubit roles were fixed in cases.json before submission, while the analysis (phase-domain residual, driver and conditioning fields, every threshold) was fixed after the data arrived; the batch's five jobs (four additivity configurations, then teleport) ran sequentially about 11 s apart, so drift between jobs is not cancelled and enters every cross-job difference; each star and each path occupies its own qubits, so every value is a per-star or per-path input and forms no device-wide average; the counts carry no state-preparation or readout correction; one 16384-shot run on one Tuna-17 calibration. The bound assumes a pure over-rotation and a readout assignment error below 1/2; Q5, Q13 and Q15 are consistent with coherent error without bounding it, and each driver's value comes from one configuration job.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/analysis.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/results.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-331.
  Acquisitions: qi-tuna17-crosstalk-teleport.
- **qi-tuna17-transported-pair-teleport** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Teleporting Pauli eigenstates through a Bell pair whose half a SWAP carries to the destination, with the Pauli-frame correction X^m(P1) Z^m(I) applied to the recorded bit, gives raw Tuna-17 fidelities 0.746 (|0>), 0.827 (|1>), 0.844 (|+>) and 0.862 (|+i>), and 0.768 to 0.864 on the post-selected m(I) = m(P1) = 0 subset at fractions 0.230 to 0.268; the QX reference reads 1.0 on both of its paths.
  Boundary: The circuits, shots and qubit roles were fixed in cases.json before submission, while the analysis (phase-domain residual, driver and conditioning fields, every threshold) was fixed after the data arrived; the batch's five jobs (four additivity configurations, then teleport) ran sequentially about 11 s apart, so drift between jobs is not cancelled and enters every cross-job difference; each star and each path occupies its own qubits, so every value is a per-star or per-path input and forms no device-wide average; the counts carry no state-preparation or readout correction; one 16384-shot run on one Tuna-17 calibration. Each input ran on its own path, so no single channel has a Bloch-sphere average and the 0.831 cross-axis mean assumes a path-independent channel; measure-and-prepare reaches the |0> and |1> rows, so only the |+> and |+i> rows test phase transport; the correction is applied in post-processing because the backend has no feed-forward, and the raw values are not comparable to a SPAM-corrected process fidelity.
  Evidence: [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/analysis.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-tuna_17/results.json), [analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-QXsinglenodesimulator/analysis.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-331.
  Acquisitions: qi-tuna17-crosstalk-teleport, qi-qx-crosstalk-teleport-reference.
- **qi-ry-emulator-bit-order** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The Quantum Inspire Ry emulator returns qubit 0 as the leftmost outcome character: an X on q[0] of a two-qubit circuit with no bit register read `10` in all 1024 shots, the reverse of QX and Tuna, which put qubit 0 rightmost. The transported-pair teleport circuit, with a bit register and measurements, failed at execution with an empty log and the message 'error interpreting cQASM script'.
  Boundary: One diagnostic circuit and one failed circuit, one run each, read after the teleport failure motivated the diagnostic; the failure's log is empty, so which construct the emulator rejects (bit register, measurement statement, H or CNOT) is not isolated, and the bit order is observed on the one-hot outcome of this two-qubit register only.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T213823Z-Rydberg-diagnostic/results.json), [x-only.cq.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T213823Z-Rydberg-diagnostic/raw/x-only.cq.json), [results.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/runs/2026-10-16T211635Z-Rydberg/results.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Acquisitions: qi-rydberg-x-diagnostic, qi-rydberg-teleport.
- **qi-tuna17-zero-divisor-common-response** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The retained 4000-draw shared-shot/readout bootstrap and an independent analytic covariance replay agree on the Tuna-17 witness: corrected B-D=0.043494 (analytic z=12.97), D+B-1=-0.042291 (z=-12.15), fixture-1/2=-0.016412 (z=-6.70), and H=fixture-(dark+bright)/2=0.004733 (z=1.61). Raw H=0.007507 (z=2.72). The pooled corrected probabilities are compatible with a common affine response at this precision; non-rejection does not establish that model. Raw fixture consistency does not survive the retained readout correction. The corner has no resolved endpoint contrast, and its corrected H=0.018366 (z=3.50) also compares a Q4 fixture selector with Q2 endpoint selectors.
  Boundary: This is secondary reanalysis, fixed after acquisition. H and its regional comparisons have no multiplicity correction; the original numerical |z|=3 benchmark does not make H preregistered. Uncertainty includes same-shot selector dependence and shared calibration sampling, but not between-job drift or calibration misspecification. Each fixture has four more CZ than the endpoints, and the weak corner also changes selector. The raw covariance methods differ by n versus n-1; corrected analytic and bootstrap standard errors differ by at most 1.4%. A probability-level affine description identifies no unique circuit channel, coupler setting, or cause of the spatial association.

- **qi-tuna17-coupler-zz-map** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Conditional Ramsey over Tuna-17's bipartite coupling map gives every parked coupler's residual ZZ: (11,14) J = -31.4 +- 0.9 kHz, measured -31.40 and -31.47 kHz from its two ends, (1,4) -19.3 kHz, (12,14) +10.4 kHz, (3,5) +11.7 kHz (one end), (0,2) -7.9 kHz, (3,6) -7.8 kHz (one end) and (11,13) +7.5 kHz, and the remaining 17 couplers lie within 0.03 to 6.6 kHz in magnitude; the 15 couplers with two usable ends agree within |z| 1.7 except (8,11) at z = -3.2; the QX dry run on the 10-qubit subgraph reads every J consistent with zero (largest |z| 1.73).
  Boundary: The data-taking design (bipartite roles, delays 0.5 and 2 us, quadratures, neighbor conditioning, readout correction) was fixed before submission; the end-quality rule (visibility at least 0.3, additivity p at least 1e-3) was fixed after the Tuna-17 data; one 16384-shot run per circuit on one calibration, the four batches minutes apart; a spectator's measured Z labels its state during the idle only up to its decay in the 2 us window, which shrinks J toward zero; neighbor readout error mixes sectors and shrinks J by about the neighbor's 1 - e01 - e10; the P-role 0.5 us and Q-role 2 us x/y quadrature pairs ran in adjacent batches minutes apart, so a calibration change between those batches enters their phases; both readout references ran in batch 00 and correct all four batches, and refitting with no readout correction moves no J by more than 0.76 standard errors; a frame change between the two delays' batches shifts omega only, while a coupler retuned between batches would enter J.
  Evidence: [coupler_zz_tuna_17_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/coupler_zz_tuna_17_analysis.json), [coupler_zz_qx_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/coupler_zz_qx_analysis.json), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-333; source regenerated by core-332.

- **qi-tuna17-q5-q6-q7-short-coherence** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). As Ramsey targets, Tuna-17 qubits 5, 6 and 7 lose visibility to 0.04, 0.27 and 0.08 by 2 us, while every other qubit keeps visibility of at least 0.75; qubits 5 and 6 idle 322 and 324 kHz from their frames (modulo the 666.7 kHz alias period of the two-delay rate) and qubit 7 at -116 kHz, and with every sector on one alias branch the pairwise-additive rate model holds on all three (chi2 16.4 on 11, 0.26 on 1 and 0.13 on 4 degrees of freedom); their couplers take J from the other end only.

- **qi-tuna17-spectator-shift-zz-split** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The coupler map accounts for the whole neighbor-drive phase shift where coupler ZZ dominates: driving Q14 moved Q11 by +0.153 rad against +0.158 +- 0.005 rad predicted from J(11,14) = -31.4 kHz with the driver excited half of the 800 ns window, and Q8 on Q4 by +0.030 against +0.025; the residuals beyond coupler ZZ are drive crosstalk of +0.618 rad for Q7 on Q4, -0.133 for Q1 on Q0, +0.133 for Q5 on Q3, +0.099 for Q2 on Q0 and +0.051 for Q13 on Q11.
  Boundary: Inference fixed after both data sets; the half-excited driver is a model of a 40-X train, not a measurement; the crosstalk run and the coupler map ran about 90 minutes apart on possibly different calibrations; the measured shifts carry about 0.011 rad of sampling error.

- **qi-tuna17-register-and-readout-state** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Tuna-17 returns the full bit register: bit[34] circuits on qubit[17] came back as 34-character strings carrying every mid-circuit outcome. On 2026-10-17, Q9's idle P1 rose from 0.02-0.05 to 0.27-0.29 from 04:06Z and stayed there through 05:33Z, and after the 04:12Z platform calibration Q0 and Q6 prepared in |1> read 0.39 and 0.14 after a 2 us wait (0.85 and 0.67 at 03:39Z) while their idle P1 stayed below 0.04.
  Boundary: Readout-calibration circuits that idle 2 us before readout, so P(0|1) folds 2 us of relaxation into the assignment error; the platform publishes no calibration, so a change is located only to the interval between two retained batches.
  Evidence: [readout_ringdown_tuna_17_device_context.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/readout_ringdown_tuna_17_device_context.json), [qx-runs.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/qx-runs.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md).

- **qi-tuna17-q9-readout-misassignment** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Q9's elevated idle P1 on Tuna-17 from 04:06Z on 2026-10-17 is misassignment of |0> that leaves the qubit in |0>. Same-batch idle and X readouts bound, at one-sided 3-sigma binomial limits, e0 >= 0.19 in the three zero-wait batches (0.145 with a 2 us wait) against e0 <= 0.030 before the change, and the excited population p <= 0.115. In series Q at zero delay, shots whose opening readout of Q9 gave 1 end an X90 pair at the level of opening-0 shots: excited fraction w = 0.021 +- 0.013 over 2122 opening-1 shots, where the other eight targets read -0.10 to 0.20.
  Boundary: The bounds assume p <= 1/2, an X that inverts at most fully and idle and X readouts from one batch; the herald test reads the zero-delay arm, because relaxation during a longer delay pulls w toward 0, and its opening readout is a mid-circuit readout taken with nine targets read at once, at 04:39Z.

- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-follow-ladder-drift** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The follow series' reported estimates and their limits on Tuna-17. Coherence ladder (CZ depths 1, 5, 9; unweighted fits, 99% shot-noise intervals): r_coh = 0.962 and 0.957, r_pop = 0.925 and 0.915, r_coh / r_pop = 1.040 and 1.047 on (1,4) and (10,13); on (12,14) r_coh = 0.908 while 2 Pc(9) - 1 is negative, so r_pop is undefined; the phase slope per CZ lies within [-0.008, 0.008] rad on every pair. The X and Y replicas of one depth-9 bright edge, which run in consecutive batches and share the selector marginal, differ by 0.024 to 0.033 on all three pairs (4 to 7 shot-noise sigma), against at most 0.014 at depth 5 and 0.007 at depth 1, so deep-circuit populations scatter between batches by about 0.03 beyond shot noise and the ladder intervals understate the uncertainty; r_coh / r_pop above 1 and Pc(12,14, depth 9) = 0.483 below 1/2 are not established. Against T0 seven and a half hours earlier (no retained record places a platform calibration between them), the bright-edge populations moved by -0.209 on (12,14) at depth 9, -0.106 on (10,13) at depth 5 and -0.042 on (1,4) at depth 9, beyond that scatter. The q13 readout-1 bracket gives e10 = 0.091 while the bright (10,13) coherence's raw -0.911 corrects to -1.105, so the all-qubit readout pair misstates q13's |1> misassignment in the witness context. The walk controls carry phases of 0.297 +- 0.010 rad (star) and 0.052 +- 0.009 rad (path) about omega. The 21 jobs executed in 243.3 s against the design's 233.6 s.
  Boundary: Descriptive summaries of the retained follow runs; nothing here was a preregistered decision. The bootstrap carries shot and readout-bracket noise only. The q13 discrepancy has two candidate mechanisms that this design does not separate: readout that depends on the state of neighboring qubits (readout-1 excites all 17) and a preparation error of readout-1's own X on q13. The walk phases identify no CZ window or detuning, since each CZ adds its residual local phase. The path omit control reads -0.119 against a limit of 0.126 and one strut branch +0.135 against 0.136: inside the preregistered null offset, though far from 0 on shot noise. The retained shot order carries a 50 Hz modulation that grows with CZ depth (claim qi-tuna17-shot-record-50hz-line), so the ladder's decay mixes decoherence with a time-averaged oscillating phase. A within-batch ladder demodulated at the line is the prospective test of r_coh / r_pop.
  Evidence: [box_kite_tuna_17_follow_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/box_kite_tuna_17_follow_analysis.json), [experiment-program.md](../notes/box_kites/experiment-program.md), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-425.
  Acquisitions: qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-00, qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-01, qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-02, qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-03, qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-04, qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-05, qi-boxkite-tuna17-follow-batch-06.
  Depends on: qi-tuna17-boxkite-t0-depth-ladder.
- **qi-tuna17-shot-record-50hz-line** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The retained per-shot outcome lists of Tuna-17 jobs carry a line at the 50 Hz grid frequency in wall-clock time. A band scan at 0.019 to 0.021 cycles per shot finds the line (whitened, p below 1e-6 after the band's look-elsewhere factor) in 72 of 159 Tuna-17 jobs and in none of 113 QX jobs, none of 51 readout circuits (readout-0, readout-1 and the context readouts, at up to 131072 shots) and none of the 8 depth-1 LCU circuits; it appears in Ramsey, CZ-ladder, walk, additivity and Walsh-exemplar circuits, with first-harmonic amplitudes in P(1) up to 0.43 (q7, ring-down Ramsey) and 0.39 (q5, coupler-ZZ Ramsey). Across the readout ring-down arms, whose circuits differ only in a wait of tau cycles of 20 ns, the peak frequency rises with tau at 50.4 +- 4.0 Hz (31 jobs, one intercept per batch): 12.6 standard errors from the 0 Hz a shot-index-locked source predicts and 0.1 from 50 Hz, which at 50 Hz puts one shot at 402.9 to 404.6 us in the ring-down circuits and 400.7 us in the follow series. In the follow series the bright-edge selectors on (1,4), (10,13) and (12,14) oscillate with amplitudes 0.19 to 0.26 at CZ depth 9, 0.09 to 0.13 at depth 5 and below 0.0175 at depth 1, in phase across the three pairs (resultant 0.996 to 1.000 per job). Within-job stationarity (16 block means per bit, the smallest per-bit chi-square tail times the bits scanned) falls below 0.01 in 11 to 33 percent of the box-kite pre-T0, T0, follow and Walsh-exemplar jobs and in 61 to 75 percent of the morning ring-down jobs. The detection statistic whitens the scanned bits by their same-shot correlation matrix, so correlated bits of one shot do not inflate it.
  Boundary: A reanalysis of retained shot order, which the platform does not document as temporal; the wall-clock slope is the evidence that it is. The mechanism is inferred, not measured: a 50 Hz modulation common to the chip that grows with the phase a circuit accumulates (wait or CZ count) fits mains-frequency interference on the qubits' frequency or the flux pulses, but no retained probe separates flux pickup from any other 50 Hz path, and the third-harmonic amplitudes do not separate a 150 Hz component from the nonlinear response to a large phase swing. A time-averaged oscillating phase lowers a job's mean contrast, so the follow ladder's decay rates and r_coh / r_pop mix decoherence with this modulation. The scan detects lines in one band only.
  Evidence: [tuna_17_shot_record_line.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/tuna_17_shot_record_line.json), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-426.

- **qi-tuna17-boxkite-ladder-drift-harmonics** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Descriptive readings of the ladder series. Against the follow series about two and a half hours earlier, the bright-edge populations rose by 0.071 (1,4), 0.092 (10,13) and 0.394 (12,14) at CZ depth 9 and by 0.003, 0.035 and 0.190 at depth 5; (12,14) at depth 9 moved from 0.483 to 0.877. The (12,14) selector at depth 9 carries a first harmonic of 0.024 and 0.026 in batches 1 and 2 and 0.0027 in batches 4 and 5, while its second harmonic stays at 0.020 to 0.022 in all four (shot-record lock-in at each job's peak). In the shot-record scan the 21 ladder jobs fall below an adjusted block-stationarity p of 0.01 in 52 percent of jobs, and none of the 9 readout circuits carries the line at 131072 shots.
  Boundary: Descriptive; nothing here was a preregistered decision. A first harmonic that vanishes while the second persists fits a static phase moving onto an extremum of the selector's response, inferred, not measured. No retained record places a platform calibration between the follow and ladder series; batch 4 waited about 30 minutes in the queue.
  Evidence: [box_kite_tuna_17_ladder_analysis.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/box_kite_tuna_17_ladder_analysis.json), [tuna_17_shot_record_line.json](../../results/quantum_inspire/tuna_17_shot_record_line.json), [tuna-17.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17.md), [tuna-17-run-design.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/tuna-17-run-design.md), [experiments.md](../notes/quantum_inspire/experiments.md).

- **qd-belenos-random-meshes-fitted-efficiency-reanalysis** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Refitting the four C1 runs with relative per-mode detection efficiencies fitted jointly with each class, each run held out in turn, cuts the held-out median TVD to 4.2 shot-noise floors (absolute TVD median 0.149 against the ideal, range 0.031 to 0.273, floor 0.034), with every class's best point at or next to its null magnitude and no class passing the fit test; static offsets fitted with the efficiencies lose to fitted dephasing on held-out runs (about -34000 nats), and the fitted efficiencies put modes 0 to 11 near 1.2 and modes 12 to 23 near 0.7, with mode 5 a 6.6-fold outlier that also collects light the ideal places on modes 0 and 4.
  Boundary: Post hoc: the efficiency model was written after job-03 had been examined in sample and committed (5ad0008) before it ran on the four runs; the efficiencies are a fitted factor per output mode and support no detector-efficiency claim. It motivates a prospective test that scores a further job against these fixed efficiencies, belenos_mesh job-02 on qpu:belenos in the same coupler state.
  Evidence: [efficiency.json](../../results/quandela/sessions/2026-10-18T030133Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh/efficiency.json), [strategy.md](../notes/quandela/strategy.md).

- **qd-belenos-light-cone-reading** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Over every retained qpu:belenos run of Q1's circuit, in_block over the one-click samples inside source 20's light cone of each served mesh (the modes its ideal propagation passes through, joined with every mode the exact dephasing channel at 0.5 rad heater and 0.1 rad coupler noise detects at 1e-3 or more) reads 0.982 to 0.995 in every seeded iterated cell on meshes 29dc6ef8 and 14a96ca6 at 1500 to 60000 samples and 0.985 in D3's single job on 14a96ca6, while the single jobs on 29dc6ef8 read 0.507, 0.518 (unseeded, 10000 samples), 0.918 (unseeded, 1500) and 0.370 (seed 20, 10000). Off-cone clicks sit mostly on modes 0, 1 and 5, come and go within one job on one mesh (0, 0 and 81 on identical 1500-sample iterations), and share no sample with an in-cone click in every cell but one (135 pairs where 148 are expected), where independent pulse-gated clicks predict up to 329 such pairs.
  Boundary: A post hoc statistic over retained data; the preregistered verdicts of the bridge and the form-versus-dwell series stand. The noise-bounded cone puts at most 1.2e-4 of the photon on modes 0, 1 and 5 at an error that already halves in_block, so those clicks need a cause other than the mesh's routing; whether acquisition is pulse-gated is unknown, so their origin (photon-derived on fixed channels, or independent noise) stays open, and no single wrong source mode reproduces them. Acquisition times are estimates, samples / (physical_perf x clock), on a reading of physical_perf no Quandela document confirms. Seeded cells sit 1 to 23 shot floors from their mesh's ideal inside the cone (TVD 0.012 to 0.228), and the bridge's 22 meshes read 0.852 to 0.998 inside the ideal cone, repeating within the job; exact heater dephasing fits that spread worse than none, and static per-mesh error classes were not scanned on these meshes.
  Evidence: [belenos_light_cone.json](../evidence/quandela/belenos_light_cone.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-17T213646Z-qpu_belenos-box_kite_photonic_single-job-04/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T025441Z-qpu_belenos-box_kite_photonic_single-job-04/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T042403Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-08/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T074524Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-09/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T090333Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-10/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T090434Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-11/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T090534Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-12/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T155323Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-13/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T190504Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-14/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T193509Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-16/raw/result.json), [strategy.md](../notes/quandela/strategy.md), [form-dwell-decomposition.md](../notes/quandela/form-dwell-decomposition.md).

- **qd-belenos-single-job-failure-is-a-transformation** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). On qpu:belenos mesh 29dc6ef8 the requested experiment and its reported mesh fail to predict the returned output distribution of the 10000-sample single jobs, which put most of the target block on its dilation modes, where the ideal is dark: Q1 (206, 996, 1772, 1724) and (260, 1130, 1770, 1878) and job-13 (425, 1123, 849, 951) on modes 20 to 23 against the ideal (1/2, 1/2, 0, 0). The label modes hold 0.12, 0.14 and 0.15 of every one-click sample (0.26, 0.28 and 0.46 of the block) against 0.81 to 0.96 of every one-click sample in the passing Q1-circuit cells on 29dc6ef8 and 14a96ca6; a uniform survival factor leaves the normalized distribution unchanged and cannot populate a dark output. Q1's two identical bodies agree with each other while job-13, whose body differs only by compilation_seed 20, returns a different wrong histogram (0.31 of its clicks on mode 16) under the same reported mesh, so the reported mesh identifier does not determine the observed behavior. The RCA's implemented error classes over their searched ranges fit job-13 no better than TVD 0.39 against a 0.012 shot floor, the ideal outputs of the 135 complete meshes served to this account no better than 0.20 (Q1) and 0.29 (job-13), and reweighting the passing 10000-sample iteration by powers of the receipt's t_out leaves it 0.77 to 0.79 from job-13.
  Boundary: Three failing single jobs on one mesh. The record does not isolate the optical transformation from input preparation, applied settings, detector assignment, acquisition, the service's default DetectorBalancing or result assembly; the fitted phase gains (-0.30 for Q1, +0.41 for job-13) characterize the failure of one gain model, not a physical gain. The stale-load test covers only complete meshes served to this account, not a partial configuration, another account's configuration, a mixture or stale load plus further error; the reweighting test excludes the t_out family only, not arbitrary correction, mode assignment or format-dependent processing. The reported mesh hash verifies the returned circuit description, not the applied phases.
  Evidence: [belenos_light_cone.json](../evidence/quandela/belenos_light_cone.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-17T213646Z-qpu_belenos-box_kite_photonic_single-job-04/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T025441Z-qpu_belenos-box_kite_photonic_single-job-04/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T090534Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-12/raw/result.json), [result.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-18T155323Z-qpu_belenos-belenos_mesh-job-13/raw/result.json), [strategy.md](../notes/quandela/strategy.md), [form-dwell-decomposition.md](../notes/quandela/form-dwell-decomposition.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-546; replay failure paths checked by core-545.

- **hw-kingston-rep-delay-timeout** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). Four ibm_kingston initialization-control jobs of 100,000 shots ended in error 1305, the service's ran-longer-than timeout, with no result: at a 0 us repetition delay (inside the configuration's rep_delay_range of 0 to 2 ms, accepted by the service's range check, below the 20 us floor of IBM's changelog) both jobs stayed running for 129 s and 207 s and reported no circuit execution time, and at 20 us both jobs ended in 1305 under a 6 s execution ceiling. The service billed 2 s for each 0 us job and 5 s for each 20 us job, 14 s in all, and the settled charges match the account inventory.
  Boundary: Code 1305 covers every ceiling cancellation in these jobs, so it does not separate a stalled shot loop from an overrun ceiling; that the 0 us jobs never started a shot loop is the inference their absent circuit execution time supports.

- **hw-kingston-init-correlation-common-mode** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). On the 149-qubit ibm_kingston initialization controls, the variance of the excited-qubit count exceeds the independent Poisson-binomial prediction by 5 to 7 percent, and the x_history excess agrees with the prep0 excess within one joint standard error at every delay (standard errors and z values from the findings document): 0.0503 against 0.0521 at 20 us, 0.0598 against 0.0594 at 50 us and 0.0623 against 0.0734 at 250 us. The excess is common to both shot histories, so it belongs to correlated readout or a common-mode event independent of the preceding shot's end state, and the independent preparation channel stands.
  Boundary: Each history difference spans a dynamic and a static job as well as the shot history. A cell pair whose x_history excess exceeds its prep0 excess by more than the joint standard error reopens a joint preparation channel.

- **hw-n21-q143-segmented-lines** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). In the segmented coprime sweep of ibm_fez qubit 143 (48 PUBs x 1536 shots, six segments per shot, every member admitted on its returned schedule and heralds), the two lines are a classical mixture fixed within each shot: an exchange-coupled two-level system fits worse by 10063 in NLL, the Z arms carry no swap memory (18.3 against 42.3) and the 1250 dt herald pre-idle moves no quadrature (3.5 against 13.7). The splitting df = -90.2 kHz lies in alias cell 0, resolved by 5350 in NLL over the next cell, with weights 0.529 / 0.471. In shot order the line labels of the half-cycle delays correlate across columns 8.934 ms apart (the metered circuit execution time per shot) and decay as a two-state telegraph with switching time tau = 26.2 ms (moving-block bootstrap 2.5 to 97.5 percent 19.3 to 30.5 ms; dwell 56 and 50 ms; 28.3 ms at the chunk's wall period, an upper bound). The raw reads of the four whole-cycle delays up to 30.5 us correlate within 0.034 at lag 1 (standard error 0.026); at 8316 dt the raw correlation 0.090 equals that arm's own label correlation 0.089, the residual line separation at 33 us.
  Boundary: The preregistered segmented-sweep.json refuses every member (the bound reader kept a barrier before INIT on the ibm_fez table); the verdicts come from the amended analyzer at closure 759d82d74c57 (correcting closure 6d59d710eb61's unconverged fits) and the switching time from a post hoc shot-order analysis, so neither confirms. The near-equal weights fixed over a shot fit charge-parity splitting, which a parity-resolved readout would test; a dispersively coupled two-level fluctuator switching in tens of ms predicts the same telegraph. Shot-to-shot correlation at 9.7 ms makes the per-shot Wald statistics anti-conservative; the non-rejections and the model and alias margins survive that.
  Evidence: [segmented-sweep-759d82d74c57.json](../../results/live/2026-10-21T064222.721454Z/segmented-sweep-759d82d74c57.json), [line-switching-759d82d74c57.json](../../results/live/2026-10-21T064222.721454Z/line-switching-759d82d74c57.json), [dense-sha256.json](../../results/live/2026-10-21T064222.721454Z/dense-sha256.json), [N21_Q143_COPRIME_SWAP_CASE.md](../../results/live/N21_Q143_COPRIME_SWAP_CASE.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-617; active code regression checked by core-619.
  Acquisitions: fez-n21-q143-segmented-sweep.
  Depends on: hw-n21-spectator-143-beat.

The verdicts come from the amended analyzer at closure 759d82d74c57, whose preconditioned fit corrects closure 6d59d710eb61's cell -2 and T2* 52 us (decay times left at their starts).

- **hw-quaternion-encoded-order-pilot** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The Tuna-17 order pilot completed 24 jobs and 393216 shots. Six native-zero dark-port controls measured 0.0396118 to 0.0508423; two native-0.25 settings measured 0.2669067 and 0.2703857. The refreshed ledger records 186.801814 execution seconds and zero credit debit.
  Boundary: One fixed chronological acquisition per science setting leaves preparation, compiled-circuit effects, readout and time drift confounded. - **qd-belenos-running-status-holds-jobs** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). While qpu:belenos read running on its Explorer row and processor record, then calibration, a one-computation job (90712931, box_kite_photonic_single job-00, 10000 samples) entered the token's queue and stayed waiting with no start time for 26 minutes; it was canceled with 0 shots and no charge (qpu:belenos usage 17.2255 before and after). The run retains no read of the public platform list in that interval, so the observation concerns the Explorer and processor status only.

- **qd-belenos-bdsc-epoch1-sum-rules-fail** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). In epoch 1 of the frozen BDSC qualification case on qpu:belenos (jobs 2524f061, b5bdf7f2, 2bc57917, 6d2dac12; 48 settings, 300000 samples each, every iteration's parameters and seed echoed, at most 0.06 % excluded by the one-click rule, 1.6 credits per job) the within-cell sum rules p(+) + p(-) = p(f) + p(g) read 9 violated, 4 inconclusive and 3 compatible of 16 at margin 0.02 on the case's simultaneous Clopper-Pearson event restricted to epoch 1. Under the empty mitigation list the target-complement pair reads f 0.002 to 0.004 and g 0.986 against ideal 0 and 1 while all eight superposition states read 0.375 to 0.437 against 0.5; the cross-template contrasts read 13 compatible, 5 violated and 6 inconclusive of 24. Only the empty-list job returned zero-click outcomes (25 to 140 per iteration), so the provider-default route processes what it returns.
  Boundary: Epoch 1 alone, read before the frozen readout: the case decides only on all 96 observations across both epochs, and admission waits on the provider-contract answers, so this is no qualification verdict. A violated sum rule says the prepared states differ from the requested ones for any fixed effect; attributing it to the compiled mesh's phases rather than the detection stage is an inference the design does not isolate.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T122531Z-qpu_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-def-e1/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T162332Z-qpu_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-none-e1/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T162656Z-qpu_belenos-bdsc_qualification-mm-def-e1/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T165036Z-qpu_belenos-bdsc_qualification-mm-none-e1/results.json), [bdsc-qualification-case.md](../runbooks/bdsc-qualification-case.md).
  Acquisitions: qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-mm-def-e1, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-mm-none-e1, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-tc-def-e1, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-tc-none-e1.

A 12-iteration template job on qpu:ascella (c2edef5d, 2026-10-21 20:34 UTC, platform reading available) ended in error 1 s after starting with failure_code RUNTIME ERROR and 'hardware_info.circuit_input_ports must be defined in the hardware_config to use Instruments.set_pic_input()', its one results_list entry, for the first of the 12 iterations (seed 0), holding that text instead of counts and the other eleven iterations returning no entry; it was charged nothing (qpu:ascella 0.0 credits for 1 job).
  Boundary: One job; the message names the instrument layer's photon-input step, which every Ascella job takes, but whether other job forms fail the same way at this time is not tested.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T203428Z-qpu_ascella-bdsc_ascella_control-job-00/results.json), [calibration.md](../notes/quandela/calibration.md).
  Acquisitions: qd-qpu-ascella-bdsc-control.
- **qd-belenos-bdsc-repeat-tc-default-shifts** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The repeat acquisition of the target-complement state pair under Quandela's default mitigation on qpu:belenos (job ee6fc975, case job tc-def-e2; submitted 2026-10-22 01:35 UTC under platform status running, started after a 42-minute queue wait, ran 92 s, 1.6 credits) returned all 12 settings at 300000 samples with at most 0.032 % excluded by the one-click rule, and the freshly built job body carried the requested policy, so the submission and collection path at collector revision f1dc1c0 works end to end. Against the first acquisition of the same job (2524f061, 2026-10-21) the basis states f and g moved by at most 0.0033 while the eight superposition states moved by -0.071 to +0.041, many times the binomial standard error of about 0.001, and the within-template sum-rule residuals p(+) + p(-) - p(f) - p(g) read -0.080 and -0.039 (outward ladder, real and imaginary pairs) and -0.032 and -0.081 (reverse ladder) against -0.035, +0.031, -0.042 and +0.000 in the first acquisition. Between the two acquisitions the published source figures moved from HOM 87.3 to 87.8 % to 80.4 %, transmittance 6.05 to 6.13 % to 5.25 % and g2 1.0 % to 0.9 %.
  Boundary: One repeat job, run as the engineering validation of the runbook's first 2026-10-22 amendment (step 5). scripts/bdsc_cases.py draws each compilation seed by observation index, epoch included, so all 12 settings ran with new seeds and returned different circuits: the shift compares matched requests, not matched compilations. The forensic reading (docs/notes/quandela/bdsc-acceptance-diagnostics.md, rated moderate) takes the 0.035 spread of the superpositions about their common drop as a per-compilation response and leaves the common drop of 0.024 unsplit between the device, its characterization and the default policy's processing. No calibration cycle between the two acquisitions is documented, so the shift supports no claim about reproducibility across a recalibration. The residuals are point differences without a simultaneous interval; the crossed diagnostic case, which replaces the suspended confirmation design, is the design that separates these components.
  Evidence: [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-22T013357Z-qpu_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-def-e2/results.json), [results.json](../../results/quandela/runs/2026-10-21T122531Z-qpu_belenos-bdsc_qualification-tc-def-e1/results.json), [bdsc-acceptance-diagnostics.md](../notes/quandela/bdsc-acceptance-diagnostics.md), [bdsc-qualification-case.md](../runbooks/bdsc-qualification-case.md).
  Gates: replayed by core-622.
  Acquisitions: qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-tc-def-e1, qd-qpu-belenos-bdsc-tc-def-e2.
- **iqm-garnet-mock-runtime-estimate** (exploratory reanalysis, hardware). The garnet:mock pipeline estimated 2.007 s for one circuit of 1000 shots, 3.227 s for one circuit of 4000 shots and for twenty circuits of 200 shots, and 1.635 s for 4000 shots with active_reset_cycles 1: a fixed 1.60 s per job plus 0.407 ms per passive-reset shot (the mock calibration's 400 us reset_wait, 1.2 us readout and 5 us end delay), no cost per extra circuit, and about 9 us per active-reset shot. Each job charged 0.00 credits, and the mock returned random bits (P(1) 0.48 to 0.51 for an X).
  Boundary: Mock estimates under the mock's own calibration; the hardware estimate, runtime and billing are the reset and billing pilot's.

The two later submissions departed from the frozen protocol: the first job's collection at 11:15:35 already met the case's stop conditions (fewer than 40000 samples, no compiled circuit), which end the four-job sequence. None of the three runs enters the diagnostic readout; block 1 was rerun from its first job under the owner's fresh approval, 'jobs approved.', given after this record was reported.
